Nine of them, 77.48 EUR each, one a month from 2024-03 to 2024-11, have no matching family row of the partner's, and in 2025-09 two of them exceed half of the partner's three by 1.00: 698.33 EUR in all that the reconciliation can't explain. The owner and the partner correct Excel at the source and export again; D-63 keeps no exception, and the two files then agree for D3c too.
- D-150 (the PM, answering D3b's question 4; with D-113 and D-139). A personal entry that the import wrote and that was then deleted in the app stays deleted: V14 keeps the deleted entry's reference in its personal ledger (deleted with the ledger, so "Delete all my data" removes it), and the next import lists the row as a conflict and doesn't write it again.
- D3b's choices, the agent's, confirmed by the PM on 2026-10-13 (D3b-accept) (D-151 to D-160). Where D-134 to D-150 and D-61 to D-89 left a choice open:
  - D-151 (D-140, what leaves the laptop). The page uploads the partner's family rows as `ID;Date;Exp. Item;Family;Side;Currency;Sum`: `Side` names the side on UNALLOCATED (`Debit` or `Credit`), whose currency and amount go with it; his accounts, counterparties, comments and `LOAN_CA` never leave. UNALLOCATED's name is read from the owner's accounts file, in any case. The server refuses the whole request (422) for any other column, a row without Family = "да" or with the item "-", naming rows by number only, and stores the upload as `partner-family-rows.csv`, whatever the browser calls it (D-143). The command line filters the same way (`PartnerFilter`).
  - D-152 (D-81, D-82, the owner's family rows). His comment goes on his own payment entry as his private note, his `Counteragent` is the payee, and an account that requires a counterparty takes `LOAN_CA` (else "Unassigned", with a warning, as for personal rows). Every family record is split 50/50 between him and the seat by percentages (D-12: the payer gets the odd cent); D-73's records are 100 % his. Settlements, the seat's records and the lumps take no comment.
  - D-153 (D-142, the references). A personal entry is `xls:<ID>` in the personal ledger, the opening balances `xls-opening:<date>`; a family record `xls:<member id of the seat whose file it comes from>:<ID>`, a conversion's two settlements `…:<ID>:<currency>`, and a lump `xls:<owner's member id>:PL:<yyyy-MM>:<currency>`.
  - D-154 (D-83). The lumps' category is `NO_DETAIL`, "Без детализации", an expense; a lump is the month-end's value of each `PL_family_*` column in the months before the month of the partner's first family row, and one that is 0 at the currency's minor unit (Excel's floating-point residue) is none. With a family budget the partner's file is required.
  - D-155 (D-68, D-74, rounding). Every amount the family budget takes is rounded half up to its currency's minor unit (a record's, a paying side's, a settlement's), and an amount of a personal row with more decimals than a posting holds is rounded half up to 4. The reconciliation explains each rule's difference from Excel by account, currency and date: `SHARES` (D-12), `MINOR_UNIT`, `FOUR_DECIMALS`. A dash alone is 0 (Excel's accounting format), and the opening balances are taken at 4 decimals, so a residue such as -1.42109E-14 is 0.
  - D-156 (a row on one account in two currencies). The same account on both sides of a row in two currencies is an exchange within that account, through FX_EXCHANGE (rule 9); in one currency it is an error. Six of the owner's rows are such.
  - D-157 (D-146, D-147, the reconciliation's points). The cutoff is the latest date of the owner's rows and of the partner's uploaded rows (the server sees only those). The points are the month-ends of the daily balance file up to the cutoff and the cutoff day itself, which is in the tail. D-147's check that the app's FAMILY_DEBT is 0 in a currency without family rows holds at the last compared month-end; every month-end shows it, since a settlement in that currency may be open in between (USD from 2022-03 to 2022-05).
  - D-158 (D-139, what can't be written over). A known row whose kind changed (an expense, a refund, an income, who paid a record, who paid a settlement, a conversion's currency) is a row error that blocks the commit, for the owner to settle in the app; a personal entry with the row's reference that this import didn't write is a conflict. D-71's settlements are the import's own: `NewSettlement.throughExchange`, which no endpoint takes.
  - D-159 (D-11, the family categories). The import finds the family budget's category by code or creates it with the categories file's name and type, and merges the owner's personal category of that code into it (`FamilyLedgerService.importCategory`); a family category that exists keeps its name. His personal rows in such a category post to the family category.
  - D-160 (time). The import refreshes the planner's statistics of the tables it fills every 500 writes (`ANALYZE`, transactional) and builds no record view while it writes: the real export's dry run took 3.4 minutes locally, against 16 without. The import page says it takes several minutes; Caddy and the api set no timeout that ends it.
  - D-159 is D-11's existing merge of a family category into the member's personal ledger, which `DataIsolationApiTests` already covers (the PM, 2026-10-13).
- D-161 (the PM). D3b was deployed (2026-10-12, `694fa3f`) before D-144 passed: the importer was proven (0 row errors, an idempotent re-import, a clean integrity check), and what failed was the data (D-149). D3b-run waits until D-144 passes.
- D-162 (the PM). The settlement forms don't offer an account that requires a counterparty, or FX_EXCHANGE, for a side; that goes to the backlog ("Later"). Only the import writes such sides for now (D-148, D-71).
- D-163 (the PM). "A member typed on `/family/new` right after a first sign-in is not created with the budget" is reproduced by an end-to-end spec (a fresh sign-in, straight to `/family/new`, a member without an account) before anything is fixed. If it doesn't reproduce in 20 runs of the spec, that is reported, the spec stays and the page doesn't change.
- D-164 (the PM). `.github/actionlint.yaml` names `ubuntu-26.04` under `self-hosted-runner.labels`, so that actionlint 1.7.7 (the version stays) exits 0 on both workflow files.
- D-165 (the owner, 2026-10-13). "Import up to": a date on the import page (empty by default) and `--up-to=yyyy-MM-dd` for `ImportRunner`. Rows of either transactions file dated after it are not imported; the report counts them per source as "after the cutoff". They are never "missing" (D-138): a row imported before and now dated after the cutoff is left alone and not reported missing. The reconciliation compares the month-ends up to the cutoff (and D-157 otherwise), and the daily files' rows after it aren't read (with D-146). Empty means the behaviour before. A later import with a later date adds the newer rows through the usual sync (D-139). D3b-run uses 2026-08-31.
- D-166 (the PM). In the export of 2026-10-13 the owner's 19 rows with IDs 6986 to 7004, all dated after 2026-08-31, swapped IDs among themselves (the same 19 rows, the IDs reassigned in date order). Accepted once: nothing with these IDs was ever imported anywhere that persists. From this export on, IDs stay as they are (D-86); the owner removes the cause in Excel.
- D-167 (the owner, 2026-10-13). D-149 is fixed at the source. The nine monthly owner rows that were income straight into FAMILY_DEBT (2024-03 to 2024-11, the money received by the partner) are now income into a personal virtual account, with the same IDs, and nine new transfer rows (IDs 7005 to 7013, the same dates) move it from that account to FAMILY_DEBT; the importer posts them as personal income plus a settlement from the owner's side. One owner row of 2025-09 was corrected by 1.00. The owner's USD month-end PL postings are removed (the PL USD column was always 0); FAMILY_DEBT_USD now holds only the real USD movements with the partner (2022-03-19 to 2022-06-04), and FAMILY_DEBT_USD + UNALLOCATED_USD + RESERVE_USD is unchanged on every day. D-147 stays as a safeguard.
- D-168 (the PM). The daily files are refused (a file error, no row imported) when any value is written in scientific notation with an exponent of zero or more (`6.90E+02`, `0.00E+00`): Excel writes that only for cells formatted as scientific, and the digits are lost. Negative exponents (float residues such as `1.13687E-12`) stay accepted. The error names the column codes and the number of cells, never values.
- D3b-accept's choices, the agent's, confirmed by the PM in D-171 (where D-163 to D-168 left a choice open):
  - D-165's edges: D-69's tail and D-83's lumps still come from every row of the files, not only those up to the date; a row known from an earlier import is never "missing" when its Excel ID is among the rows after the date, or its stored date is after it; the daily files and the reconciliation stop at the earlier of the date and D-146's cutoff; the report's `afterCutoff` counts each file's rows (`OWNER`, `PARTNER`); a cutoff inside an open settlement in a currency without family rows fails D-147's check there, as D-157 says. Commit is enabled only for exactly the choice its dry run read, the date included.
  - D-168's message names a lump's column as `PL_family_…_CUR` (D-143), and counts the cells of the whole file, after the cutoff too.
  - D-163's spec runs locally only (production's accounts sign in once per run, D-52) and is `skipped` in production; the runbook's step 10.3 adds the partner on the Members page after creating the budget, and checks it there.
  - D3b-accept has no `deploy/checks/D3b-accept.*`: its checklist runs and verifies stage `D3b`, whose checks still hold.
- D-169 (the PM, after D3b-accept's run). The owner types each month's half of the partner's family incomes that D-84 leaves to his own rows by hand, rounded to a cent up or down (2026: -0.005, +0.005, +0.005, +0.005, -0.005, 0, +0.005, 0), which with the shares' own rounding left 0.015 at 3 EUR month-ends. Per month and currency, the Reconciler computes OWNER_HALF = the sum of the owner's rows that D-84 leaves out less half of the partner's family incomes of that month, exactly. It is an explained difference (`Drift` reason `OWNER_HALF`, accumulated like the others) when its absolute value is at most 0.005 × the number of the partner's family incomes in that month; otherwise the whole month's OWNER_HALF stays unexplained. Explained, not fixed in Excel.
- D-170 (the PM). "Create family budget" adds a name typed in "Name of a member" but not added with "Add member", as a member without an account, exactly as if Add member had been pressed (trimmed, the same validation).
- D-171 (the PM). D3b-accept's choices are confirmed: D-165's edges, D-168's message, D-163's spec local only, step 10.3, no `deploy/checks/D3b-accept.*`. USD reconciles per account without D-147 (`FAMILY_DEBT_USD` at all 107 month-ends; UNALLOCATED and RESERVE as one sum by D-67), so D-147 stays only as a safeguard.
- D3b-accept-fix's choices, the agent's, confirmed by the PM in D-172:
  - D-169's "partner's family incomes" are those D-84's rows carry: incomes in BENEFIT, OTHERINC or CASHBACK, in EUR (D-84's categories and currency), imported as records; the others are in the monthly lump. Each explained month's drift is on its latest date of either side's rows, FAMILY_DEBT +OWNER_HALF and UNALLOCATED −OWNER_HALF (Excel's debt moved by the owner's rows, the partner's records assume the exact half). The reconciliation lists every month in `ownerHalf` (the owner's rows, the partner's incomes, their number, OWNER_HALF, explained or not).
  - D-170: a typed name that Add member would refuse (one already taken) stops "Create family budget" at the field's message, rather than being dropped; with custom percentages a typed name sets them back to equal, as Add member does.
- D-172 (the PM, 2026-10-14). D3b-accept-fix's choices are confirmed: D-169 counts only the incomes in BENEFIT, OTHERINC and CASHBACK in EUR, its drift is on each month's latest date, and the report lists every month in `ownerHalf`; D-170 stops at a typed name Add member would refuse, and a typed name sets custom shares back to equal. The partner is "he" and "his" throughout this document and the docs. (D3b-doc looked for a "her" naming the partner and found none in a tracked file: every "she" and "her" there names a test persona, such as Alice or Carol.)
- D-173 (the PM, 2026-10-14). Before D3b-run's dry run, and before every later import, the owner makes sure the browser is signed in as himself, not as an end-to-end test account (`e2e-a`, `e2e-b`): the import goes into the signed-in account's ledger. A private window, signed in fresh, is the safe way. The runbook's step 10.5 says so.

Added in D3c (2026-10-15)
- D-174 (the owner, 2026-10-14). The partner's join date is 2019-12-01, the first day of his Excel.
  - Claiming the seat posts into his personal ledger an opening entry per currency (D-31) with the seat's family-debt balance at the end of 2019-11-30, then his shares of every family record from 2019-12-01 on.
  - Every record the seat paid is dated 2019-12-01 or later, so each stays open for his payment account (D-32).
  - Not the budget's start: that posts two years of shares before his Excel exists, with nothing to reconcile them against. Not the day of the claim: no payment account could ever be named on past records.
- D-175 (the owner, 2026-10-14). Once the seat is claimed, each member imports only his own file.
  - The owner's import no longer takes the partner's transactions file. That field exists only while the budget has an unclaimed seat; a request that sends it for a claimed seat is refused with a clear message.
  - The owner's import is not refused because another member has an account (D3c-0: it was).
  - The member's import posts his personal rows into his personal ledger and attaches his payment side to the family records that came from his file.
  - FAMILY_DEBT is reconciled per currency only up to the earlier of the two members' latest committed cutoffs. For a seat before its claim, its cutoff is that of the owner's import that uploaded its rows; after the claim, until the member imports himself, it stays that same cutoff. Later month-ends are reported as "waiting for the other member's import", not as failed.
- D-176 (the owner, 2026-10-14). The records that came from the partner's file (imported for the seat by D3b-run) become his with the claim, as author and payer.
  - His are the family fields (category, split, comment, amount, date), the payment side and delete; his re-imports update them.
  - Records that came from the owner's file and were paid by the seat stay the owner's as author; the partner gets only their payment side.
  - The owner sees the partner's records as any member's (D-14, D-88).
- D-177 (the owner, 2026-10-15; replaces the version of 2026-10-14). The partner's files now have the owner's layout.
  - The member's import reads his transactions file with the owner's parser and requires the same columns, CODE_ITEM, CUR_FAMILY, FAMILY_EXP and FAMILY_INC included. A file without them is refused with a message naming the missing columns. His daily files are read as the owner's are.
  - His CODE_ITEM is checked against the categories file as the owner's is. In export-2026-10-15 every item matches, and his COUNT_DEBIT and COUNT_CREDIT match his accounts file.
  - Family shares still come from the budget's records. His CUR_FAMILY must equal the family record's currency: a difference is a row error. His FAMILY_EXP or FAMILY_INC must equal his share of the record within D-180's tolerance: a difference is a warning with the Excel ID.
  - Every reader takes columns by header name, never by position. The owner's upload of a seat's family rows (D-151's seven columns) works with either layout of the partner's file.
  - His upload: his accounts file, the categories file (the same CashFlowItems the owner uploads), his transactions file, his daily balance file and his daily change file.
- D-178 (the owner, 2026-10-14; refined by the PM 2026-10-15). His settlement rows (FAMILY_DEBT against an account of his) never create settlements.
  - Each is matched to an existing settlement of the same currency and direction, in this order: (1) the same date and amount; (2) one row of his against several of the owner's on the same date whose sum equals it (2025-06-12 has such a case); (3) the same amount within ±7 days, when the pair is unambiguous.
  - His FAMILY_DEBT conversions are matched to the owner's conversions by both legs (the currency and amount of each side), whichever currency the row's Sum is written in. The 2022-06-05 conversion is written in EUR on his side and in USD on the owner's.
  - The matched settlement gets his account as the partner's side.
  - His unmatched settlement rows are row errors: Excel ID, date, currency, amount, "no counterpart".
  - The owner's settlements from 2019-12-01 on that end up without his side are warnings.
  - The Family flag on a transfer row is ignored: 38 of his transfer rows are marked family, 4 of them settlements.
- D-179 (the owner, 2026-10-14). His rows between FAMILY_DEBT and UNALLOCATED:
  - Income straight into FAMILY_DEBT (his benefit rows, 187 up to 2026-08-31) is not posted. It is his share of the family incomes another member received, which the claim already posts. The import checks it per month and currency against his shares of the family income records paid by other members (D-180's tolerance), and lists every month that differs as a warning, with both sums. Expected with export-2026-10-15: no warning (the 2025 rows 9505 and 9693 are fixed, and with full precision the 2021 half-cents match too).
  - An expense paid straight from FAMILY_DEBT (4 rows up to 2026-08-31, Excel IDs 836, 2788, 3530, 6913) is a row error: "split it in Excel into a settlement into an account of yours and the expense from that account".
- D-180 (the owner, 2026-10-14). The member's reconciliation uses a 0.01 tolerance per account, currency and month-end, since his daily balance file may hold 2 decimals.
  - FAMILY_DEBT is compared as movement since his first day. The difference between the claim's opening and his daily balance file at 2019-11-30 is reported once as an explained difference (D3c-0: about −0.03 in RUB).
  - The two 2025 EUR steps are fixed in Excel (his Excel IDs 9505 and 9693 in export-2026-10-15), and his daily balance file must carry them. They are not covered by the tolerance.
- D-181 (the owner, 2026-10-14). His family rows keep the stored hash over the 7 columns D-151 uploaded.
  - His payment side (account, currency and amount columns, LOAN_CA) gets its own hash.
  - A first import of his own file leaves the family records unchanged and reports "payment side attached", except D-190's precision edits.
  - Later edits of either part are detected and reported separately.
- D-182 (the PM). The claim is atomic: everything it posts commits together or not at all, and a failed claim leaves the seat unclaimed. The page waits with a message and doesn't allow a second click. If the request can't reliably outlive the timeouts on its way (Caddy, the browser, the session), it runs as a background job with a status the page polls; that is an agent's choice, reported with its reason, as is the claim's time on the local copy (D3c-0: 178 s from 2017-11-01).
- D-183 (the PM). The owner sets the join date when he invites someone to a seat, and the default is the budget's start. The invite page and the accept page show it, and the accept page says how many records will be attached. If F5 already decides this differently and safely, F5's way stays and is reported.
- D-184 (the owner, 2026-10-14). D3c-0's open item is closed. The 296 entries, 107 counterparties, 14 accounts beyond the starter ones and the extra categories in the 10.2 restore test were the owner's demo data. During step 10.3 he deleted them with "Delete all my data", which also deleted the budget he had just created; he created it again before 10.4. Corrected by the owner in D3c-fix2 (2026-10-16): he doesn't recognise the 296 entries, 107 counterparties and 14 accounts; they aren't his. D3c-run's step 11.3 runs `deploy/checks/D184.sql`'s query, read only, to see whose such rows the database holds now. The runbook's D3b-run row records it, and D3c checks locally whether "Load demo data" adds exactly those counts. D3c's check (2026-10-15, the api of D3c on an empty ledger, the family switch on): it doesn't. "Load demo data" adds 164 entries, 10 counterparties, 3 accounts beyond the starter ones (the demo's credit card and dollar account, and the demo family budget's debt account), 3 categories and the family budget "Demo household"; the 296, 107 and 14 were something else (D3c-0's guess: an earlier test import of his chart), for the owner to say. D3c-fix2's try of the query on a database rebuilt from D3b-run's replay: one user, the import's, with 14 accounts beyond the starter ones (none of them the demo's) and one import batch; so an import of the owner's chart of accounts adds exactly 14, which fits D3c-0's guess but doesn't prove it.
- D-185 (the PM). "Delete all my data" also deletes budgets, so the runbook's empty-ledger check comes before the budget is created (step 10's order for any later first import). In D3c-run it comes before the partner accepts the invite.
- D-186 (the PM). After D-176 the partner authors about 7800 family records. "Delete all my data" and leaving a family budget, by either member, must never delete or change a family record that another active member's ledger posts. If today's code could do so, the button and the leave action refuse in that case with a message; closing or leaving a shared budget goes to the backlog. The end-to-end suite's cleanup keeps leaving no data, removing the accounts' data in an order the rule allows if it must.
- D-187 (the PM). The partner sees and changes the counterparties on the records that pass to him, and the owner keeps all of his own unchanged. If D3b stored them under the owner, the claim gives the partner his own copies (by name, once each) and points his records to them; if the model already makes this unnecessary, it stays and is reported.
- D-188 (the PM). The partner's side of a matched settlement is posted on the settlement's date, as the model posts it. The reconciliation counts the matched pairs that straddle a month-end; each one's amount at that month-end is an explained difference, listed.
- D-189 (the PM). D3c's local real-data run and its pass criteria are those of D3c's task, item 7 (data/private/reports/D3c.md).
- D-190 (the PM). The partner's export carries full precision from export-2026-10-15 on.
  - Rows whose uploaded columns changed only in precision are ordinary edits: the next import that uploads them updates them and lists them. No special case.
  - A Sum of "-" and a Sum of 0 mean the same: the row is skipped as zero, as D3b did.
  - Up to 2026-08-31, ten of his family rows changed this way: 3273, 3274, 3784, 3785 and 4814 (SUM_DEBIT); 6242, 6243 and 9916 (Sum); 6402 and 6606 (Sum "-" to 0, skipped both times). Which of them count as edits depends on D-151's seven columns.
- D-191 (the PM). Every local real-data run that prepares a production step first replays production's history: the imports production has had, with the same exports, cutoffs and order. Today that is D3b-run, export-2026-10-13 up to 2026-08-31. Only then comes the step under test, with the newest export. D3c's item 7 and the runbook's step 11.1 follow it.
- D3c's choices, the agent's, confirmed by the PM in D3c-fix (2026-10-15). Where D-174 to D-191 left a choice open:
  - The claim (D-182) stays one request, in the one transaction F5's accept already was: no background job. Caddy and the api set no timeout that ends a request (D-160; D3b-run's imports of about 4 minutes ran so in production), the browser's fetch has none, and the session and its token are read when the request starts. Locally the claim from 2019-12-01 took 184 s (11381 records). The page says it is taking the place and waits; the Accept button is disabled, and a second submit (Enter in the name field) sends nothing either.
  - D-183: F5's way stays. The owner picks the claim's join date on the members page, today by default (a date the owner leaves as it is posts nothing historical); 11.4 types 2019-12-01. The owners' list and the accept page show the date; the lookup adds `recordsAttached`, the records from the join date that name the place, which the accept page states.
  - D-186: today's code never deletes or changes what another active member's ledger posts, so neither the button nor leaving refuses anything new. Leaving: the last owner is refused (`LAST_OWNER`) while another member with an account remains; anyone else is detached, and the records that involve them freeze (D-19). "Delete all my data": the member becomes FORMER, their own family comments are erased (D-20, not posted anywhere), and the budget is deleted only when no other ACTIVE member with an account remains (D-36). The end-to-end cleanup is unchanged; the new spec deletes A's data and then B's.
  - D-187: nothing to copy. The place's records carry no counterparty: a record's payee and an account's counterparty are on the payer's own payment entry (D-110, D-111), which a place without an account never had, and the owner's stay on his own entries. The partner's import puts his own (Counteragent as the payee, LOAN_CA as an account's counterparty) on his entries.
  - The member's import is the request that names the uploader's own place, which must be a place he took by a claim; his new family records are split with the one other ACTIVE member (otherwise 409). A member who isn't an owner can't name another place (409). Of his rows, one with FAMILY_DEBT on both sides is a conversion, one between FAMILY_DEBT and UNALLOCATED a benefit row or D-179's error, one between FAMILY_DEBT and another account a settlement, whatever the Family flag says; a family row with an item that no import wrote yet is created, paid by him and split 50/50, as the owner's import made them.
  - His payment side's hash (D-181) covers, besides the accounts, currencies, amounts and LOAN_CA, the Counteragent and Comment that become his payee and private note, so that an edit of either shows. With it the app's side right after the import is hashed: a later change in Excel overwrites the side only if the app's is unchanged, otherwise it is a conflict.
  - V15 holds what the import needs: each seat's latest committed cutoff per family budget, and his imported sides in his personal ledger (deleted with it). A seat without a stored cutoff (D3b-run predates V15) limits nothing; the next committed import of its file stores one.
  - After the claim, the owner's import finds the months of D-83's lumps from the place's earliest imported record, D-169's partner incomes from the place's records up to the cutoff, D-12's cut of the place's records against Excel's exact halves from those records (the explanation his upload of the partner's rows gave before; the local run found the owner's reconciliation failing without it), and the family currencies from the records he is in as well as from his file.
  - D-178's tiers: the second takes one set of 2 to 6 settlements that add up, when it is the only one, and needs the row's account in their currency; a conversion matches when both legs agree in currency, amount and direction within 7 days and no other conversion does. Only settlements whose side of his is still on "Specify later" are matched.
  - D-180's difference at the claim's opening is reported as CLAIM_OPENING less D-12's cut of his shares before the join date, which is reported as SHARES (locally RUB −0.385 and −0.025); the waiting of D-175 applies to FAMILY_DEBT (and D-147's sum with it) only.
  - D-177's check of FAMILY_EXP or FAMILY_INC compares their absolute value with his share's.


Added in D3c-fix (2026-10-15; D-192 to D-196 the PM's proposals, confirmed by the owner in D3c-fix2)
- D-192 (the PM's proposal, confirmed by the owner). A row whose two sides are the same account in one currency (D-156) moves nothing when the Family flag isn't set on it and both sides hold the same amount. In any import, the owner's or a member's, such a row is skipped as a no-op and listed as a warning (`SAME_ACCOUNT_NOOP`) with its ID; a row with the flag, or with two different amounts, stays an error. D3c-fix checked the 76 rows of the partner's file that D3c refused this way: all 76 have no Family flag, and on each row's date his daily change file moves the row's column by his other rows only.
- D-193 (the PM's proposal, confirmed by the owner). Item 7's 0.05 limit on the claim's opening applies to the residual after D-12's part. The report keeps both: D-12's part as SHARES, the residual as CLAIM_OPENING.
- D-194 (the PM's proposal, confirmed by the owner). The 0.01 tolerance stays. The four FAMILY_DEBT EUR month-ends of D3c's local run (2022-01-31, 2022-02-28, 2022-05-31, 2022-07-31, 0.012 to 0.015 off): their cause is found; if it is D-12's rounding, the reconciler attributes it to SHARES and compares only the residual with 0.01; otherwise the rows that cause each difference are listed. D3c-fix found it isn't D-12's rounding: each of the four is +0.01 plus Excel's balance cut to cents by the reconciler (D-63; 0.0024 at the first two, 0.0050 at the other two). The +0.01 comes from two settlement mismatches that the run's copy (his file without the refused rows) leaves visible: the owner's settlement 3610 (December 2021, a cent, no row of his) and his row 4520 against the owner's 3997 (September 2022, a cent apart, D-178 refused his). Both are in D-197's fix list; the reconciler is unchanged.
- D-195 (the PM's proposal, confirmed by the owner). "Delete all my data" erasing the member's own family comments (D-20) is not "changing a record" for D-186. D-20 stays as it is.
- D-196 (the PM's proposal, confirmed by the owner). After a claim, the owner's D-73 row (an expense the place paid for him alone) is refused in his import with a clear reason: the partner records it from his own file. None exist up to 2026-08-31.
- D-197 (the PM). The fix list for the two Excel ledgers is written to data/private/reports/D3c-fixes.md (git-ignored): the partner's rows without a counterpart side by side with the owner's settlements without his side, with proposed pairs and edits; his expenses straight from FAMILY_DEBT with the edit D-179 asks for; and a plain table (ID, file, column, current value, proposed value). No data is changed.


Added in D3c-fix2 (2026-10-16; D-198 and D-199 the PM's proposals, which the owner may still correct)
- D-198 (the PM's proposal). D-178's second tier also works the other way round: a set of 2 to 6 of the partner's settlement rows on one date that adds up exactly to one of the owner's settlements in the same currency and direction is matched to it, only when it is the only such set and the settlement's side of his is still unassigned. The same rules as the tier, mirrored; no wider date window. His side of a settlement is one, on one account: the rows must be on one account in the settlement's currency (else each is an error naming why); the side keeps the rows' Excel IDs together (`xls:<ID>+<ID>`) and their hashes as one, so that a re-import recognises them.
- D-199 (the PM's proposal). The reconciliation compares the app's exact balance with Excel's balance as read, without D-63's cut to cents; the tolerance stays 0.01.

Added in D3c-accept (2026-10-21; both confirmed by the owner in D3c-accept2)
- D-200 (the PM's proposal, confirmed by the owner). After step 11.1's run, an extra dry run of both members' imports with "Import up to" 2026-09-30, for information only: refused rows, conflicts and the reconciliation's result. It is not a criterion. D3c-accept's run stopped before it.
- D-201 (proposed in D3c-accept, confirmed by the owner). When the owner's export corrects his own settlement rows (D-197's fix list, export-2026-10-21), he applies it before the claim, in this order: he deletes in the app the family records of his rows that the corrected file no longer makes (the rows his dry run lists as missing, "no longer in the files", D-138: on export-2026-10-21 his Excel IDs 2888, 3610, 4238, 4239, 5246 and 5792), then imports the export with the partner's file and the same "Import up to" (dry run, commit, the same again), and only then invites the partner to his place. Reason: the import never deletes (D-139), and his settlements must be final before the partner's sides attach, after which D-28 locks their date, amount and deletion.
- D-197, as the owner applied it (confirmed in D3c-accept2): his change of 2888 (from FAMILY_DEBT to his own account, with the new settlement 7068) was intentional and is part of the fix for the partner's 1725; and 4239, 5246 and 5792 stay in his Excel with Sum 0, where the list said to remove them: the import skips a row with Sum 0 (D-72), and he may remove them from Excel later. Their records in the app are deleted by D-201.

Added in D3c-accept2 (2026-10-21; D-202 the PM's proposal, which the owner may still correct)
- D-202 (the PM's proposal). A date, in one currency and direction, on which two or more of the partner's settlement rows have the amount of one of the owner's settlements (D-178's first tier) is matched as a whole, never by taking the first row by Excel ID: of every assignment of that date's still-unassigned rows of his and settlements by the first tier, the second (a row in its account's own currency) and D-198 (only rows on one account, in the settlement's currency, with one counterparty), the one that covers the most of his rows is taken when it is the only one. Assignments that differ only by rows of his of one amount on one side, or by settlements of one amount, are one (they put the same amounts on the same accounts). With two or more, every row and settlement they cover is refused as AMBIGUOUS_SETTLEMENT, naming his Excel IDs and the owner's settlements (their Excel IDs where the owner's import wrote them); neither counts as without a counterpart, and those rows don't go on to the third tier. Bound: a tied date with more than 12 rows of his or more than 12 settlements, or more than 100000 assignments that cover the most rows, is ambiguous as a whole. A date without such a tie goes through the tiers as before. The matcher runs only in a member's own import, so D-202 changes nothing up to the claim. The report counts the rows it matched (`byDate`, also in their tier) and those refused (`ambiguous`).

Added in D3c-doc (2026-10-24; D-202 to D-204 confirmed by the owner, D-205 and D-206 the PM's proposals, which the owner may still correct)
- D-202, confirmed by the owner (2026-10-24), with assignments that differ only by interchangeable rows or settlements of one amount counted as one, as recorded and built in D3c-accept2.
- D-203 (confirmed by the owner). When the best assignments of a tied date (D-202) disagree, every row and settlement they cover is refused (`AMBIGUOUS_SETTLEMENT`), never one of them taken. D-202 as built does this; nothing changes.
- D-204 (confirmed by the owner). September (D-200) is checked on a throwaway database before the first production import with a later "Import up to": production's state replayed (D-191), then both members' imports committed up to the new cutoff in the order production would run them, with both reconciliations. D3c-doc did it for 2026-09-30 on `export-2026-10-21` (data/private/reports/D3c-doc.md): the owner's import, then the partner's, then the owner's dry run again; 0 refused rows, 0 conflicts, both reconciliations Passed, both imports again 0 created and 0 updated, both integrity checks `[]`. D-200's differences were the order and nothing else: his 6 September settlement rows find the owner's 6 September settlements once the owner's import has committed them, and the owner's EQUITY EUR and RUB at 2026-09-30 differ by the owner's shares of the partner's 141 September records, which only the partner's own import brings (D-175): RUB 1224.50 to the cent, EUR 167.67, of which 0.345 is D-12's rounding that the reconciliation explains as SHARES. Another export or a later cutoff needs the check again.
- D-184 closed (2026-10-24). As of D3c-run's step 11.3 no user holds demo-like or unexplained rows: the owner's ledger holds his import only, and the three other users nothing (the partner, and two accounts that are not the end-to-end ones: those leave no row after a run, and production had 3 users before and after every run of the suite). Whose the 296 entries, 107 counterparties and 14 accounts of the 10.2 restore test were is unknown; they are gone, and nothing is to be done. The query's `e2e false` for those two was right; D3c-doc's version of `deploy/checks/D184.sql` also prints each personal ledger's creation day and whether the user has a `users` row, compares the email without regard to case, and was tried locally with two accounts shaped like e2e-a and e2e-b (`e2e true` while they hold rows, no line after the suite's cleanup).
- D-205 (the PM's proposal, which the owner may correct). Every query of a runbook step that checks a user by email (step 11's kind) first prints `users with this email N`, expected 1, and the step says to stop otherwise: without it, a mistyped email or an account the app hasn't seen yet reads as an empty account. The email is compared without regard to case. Applied to step 11.3's and 11.5's queries.
- D-206 (the PM's proposal, which the owner may correct). Before an import's first check, the checklist states the file rows the import page should show and says to stop if they differ. On `export-2026-10-21`, verified from the files and with the page's own filter: the owner's transactions 7068 rows, the partner's 12762, of which 8033 are family rows. The page shows the partner's file before any check ("8033 of his 12762 rows are family rows and will be uploaded", on the owner's page while the place has no account); the owner's own file, and a member's own file on his page, appear only in the report of the first check ("After it, neither imported nor missing: 93 rows of 7068 of yours", a dry run, which saves nothing), so that line is the one compared there, before any commit. On 2026-10-24 a first dry run of 11.2a took `export-2026-10-13`'s transactions (7013 and 12651 rows) by mistake; D-206 catches that before the reconciliation does. Applied to step 11.2a, 11.6 and 11.11.

Added in D3d-doc (2026-10-24; decided by the PM, as D3d-doc's task states them)
- D-207 (the PM). The import page counting the owner's own transactions file before a check is a later change of the code, outside D3d-doc. Until then D-206's count for that file is checked on the laptop before the check (the runbook's step 12.0, which counts the rows as the import does) and against the report's line after the first dry run.
- D-208 (the PM). A checklist lists the reconciliation failures expected after the owner's import of a new month: account, currency, date and amount, exactly as the reconciliation shows them (the import page's Account, Failing month-ends, From and Worst).
- D-209 (the PM). A failure is expected only if its account, currency, date and amount all match a listed one, the amount within 0.01 (D-199's tolerance). Anything else stops the step: a failure not listed, a listed one missing, a different amount.
- D-210 (the PM). Step 12 takes three production backups, each with its restore test: before the owner's import, after it (before the partner's), and after the owner's final dry run; the step names the dump to restore for each point where it can stop.
- D-212 (the PM). Step 12's figures hold only for the export they were rehearsed with (D-204). A gate at its top compares both participants' files with the rehearsed ones and stops on any difference, which means rehearsing again first. The step's procedure is kept apart from its figures, so that each month replaces only the figures.

Added in D3e (2026-10-24; decided by the PM, as D3e's task states them)
- D-211 (the PM). Docs-only commits stay on `feature/family-budget` and are pushed; they reach `main` with the next code stage; no deploy for them.
- D-213 (the PM). The import page's amounts are the rule for a checklist's expected failure list (D-208): the Worst the page shows, never an amount worked out by hand. D3d-doc's case: the owner's share of the partner's September records was EUR 167.67, and the page's line is 167.328088, since the reconciliation explains 0.341912 of it as D-12's rounding.
- D-214 (the PM). A tool restores a production dump into a throwaway database and maps the two participants' Keycloak subs onto dev users, and nothing else, so that they sign in locally: `scripts/rehearsal-db.sh` (D3e), its data on tmpfs.
- D-215 (the PM). D-205's check finds the participants without anyone typing an email: by the family budget's members with an account, as step 12.1's query does; it still prints `users with this email N` for each.
- D-216 (the PM, deferred; worded in D3e-doc). The backup prints the ready restore line for its own dump. Deferred: `pg-backup` lives in the auth server's repository, which owns `pg-backup` and `pg-restore-test`; it is no stage of this repository's.
- D-217 (the PM). Step 12's commands live in the runbook (`deploy/RUNBOOK.md`, step 12), with the month's figures beside them; a report or a checklist refers to them and doesn't copy them.
- D-218 (the PM, at step 12's first run, 2026-10-24). After the owner's import showed three failing reconciliation lines where two were expected, the partner's phase went ahead: the difference touched only the owner's personal entries. The cause given then, a row mapped by an older code that every import calls unchanged, was wrong: D3e found that the owner's rows 211 and 2839 were changed in the app on 2026-10-14 (version 1, imported 0) while their file rows stayed as imported, and D3b-run's code had mapped them exactly as the current code does (data/private/reports/D3e-part1.md). The lines had shown since D3c-run's 11.2a.
- D-219 (the PM, D3e's proposal 2). For every row an earlier import wrote, both participants', personal entries and family records alike, the import first compares what the current code would write with what the ledger holds; then the hash and the versions:
  1. Equal: unchanged; if the stored hash differs, the hash is stored again (re-stamped), with no new version and no journal row.
  2. Not equal, the file row as imported last, the entry or record changed in the app since (its version isn't the imported one): "changed in the app, the file unchanged", listed by Excel ID. Not a conflict, never written over, no STOP by itself.
  3. Not equal, the file row as imported last, untouched in the app: re-planned, written over as an edited row, its import version moved on; listed as "re-planned: the current code maps it differently".
  4. The file row changed: written over if the app didn't change it, a conflict if it did, as before (D-139).
  5. After the claim, the owner's D-73 rows and the lumps before the partner's ledger (D-83, D-196): "can't be re-planned after the claim", listed, never written; one whose row changed is an error, as before. On production's B3: 30 rows (5 D-73 rows and 25 lumps; D3e's Part 1 counted 31 by mistake).
  A personal entry is compared on its date, kind, payee, note and each posting in order (account, currency, amount, category, counterparty). A family record is compared on what changes its version: type and sign, date, currency, amount, category, payer, payee and split; its paying side isn't, since a member changes his own account without a new version, and a member's side has D-181's hashes. D-190 amended: a row changed only in precision that makes the same record is re-stamped, not edited.
- D-220 (the PM). Step 12's local rehearsal starts from production's latest dump (D-214's tool), not from a replay of every import production has had (D-191, D-204).
- D-221 (the PM). The import page shows the participant's own transactions file's row count as soon as the file is chosen, before any check, counted as the import counts rows (every line with a value, less the header). Step 12's D-206 check reads it. Since D3e, after a commit, Commit stays off until a file or the date changes, and the report's header names a check or a commit.
- D-222 (the PM). Each of step 12's checkpoints is closed by the page's text or the command's output, pasted; a confirmation in words closes none (step 12's first run: three Commit presses reported as checks, D-218).
- D-223 (the PM). The reconciliation explains what a "changed in the app, the file unchanged" personal entry moves on the balances: what the ledger holds less what the row maps to, per account and currency, on each one's date, as APP_EDITED, listed with the Excel IDs and amounts, like SHARES and OWNER_HALF. Unexplained differences still fail. On B3 the owner's reconciliation passes, 3240 points, with 211's 2.50 RUB on EQUITY and LOANS_ASSET from 2018-01-31 explained. A family record of that group is listed, and its effect isn't worked out (none exists today), so a difference it makes fails the reconciliation.
- Personal twins of the family's categories (the PM, D3e's proposal 3). Every category code the family budget has already maps to its family category in the import, whichever rows use it; a personal category of such a code and type that no posting uses merges into it (D-11), which a dry run reports and a commit does; one that postings use stays, with a warning. Production's 12.4 commit made three such twins (OTHERINC, CASHBACK, CHARITY), unused; the next import merges them.
- D-224 (the PM). No data step follows D3e's deploy: the twins' merge and any re-stamping happen at the next monthly import (October, step 12), rehearsed from production's dump (D-220). D3e's checklist ends with the end-to-end suite and `finish`, as usual.

Added in D3e-doc (2026-10-25; decided by the PM)
- D-225 (the PM). A rehearsal database is a production dump restored on tmpfs (`scripts/rehearsal-db.sh`, D-214, D-220); that is the standard method for every rehearsal from production's data.
- D-226 (the PM). A deploy checklist in `deploy/RUNBOOK.md` keeps `<M>` in its blocks, since a commit can't name its own hash; the PM gives the blocks with M filled in, in the chat.

Added in D4a (2026-10-25; D-227 to D-243 decided by the PM, D-244 to D-252 the agent's choices)
- D-227 (the PM). The order: D4a, the export, first; D4b, the import from a single transactions file, second.
- D-228 (the PM; D4b, recorded only). Accounts and categories come from the transaction file's columns: the code from CODE_DEBIT, CODE_CREDIT and CODE_ITEM, the name from Debit, Credit and Exp. Item, the type from TYPE_DEBIT, TYPE_CREDIT and TYPE; the account and currency pairs from COUNT_DEBIT and COUNT_CREDIT.
- D-229 (the PM; D4b, recorded only). A code the app doesn't have is created automatically and listed in the dry run and the result. The whole file is refused before any write if a code has two names or two types within the file, or if a code exists in the app with another type.
- D-230 (the PM; D4b, recorded only; superseded by D-254). Names and order live in the app. A name from the file is used only when an account or category is created; an existing name is never overwritten, and a different one is an info line. New items go to the end, in the order of their first appearance by ID.
- D-231 (the PM; D4b, recorded only; its final shape is fixed in D4b after D4a's analysis). The reconciliation uses the transaction file only, no second file: (a) per row, what the code builds against the row's own DEBIT_CHANGE, CREDIT_CHANGE, FAMILY_EXP and FAMILY_INC; (b) per month-end, the ledger against the file, D3e's explanations (APP_EDITED and the others) unchanged; (c) integrity: gaps in the IDs are a warning, and IDs the ledger has that the file lacks follow the current rules.
- D-232 (the PM; D4b, recorded only). The new import neither reads nor creates the monthly PL_family sums; the lumps already imported stay as they are (D-196).
- D-233 (the PM; D4b, recorded only). Both members' opening balances are in the ledger already, and the new import doesn't create them; for a future user they are ordinary rows of the file.
- D-234 (the PM; D4b, recorded only). D4b removes the five-file import (no mode beside the new one), once a rehearsal shows that the old and the new reconciliation give the same list of differences.
- D-235 (the PM). The export is one file, Transactions, with all 32 columns in the import's header order: ID;Date;Exp. Item;Sum;Currency;Counteragent;Comment;Debit;Credit;Family;CUR_DEBIT;SUM_DEBIT;CUR_CREDIT;SUM_CREDIT;LOAN_CA;CODE_ITEM;TYPE;CODE_DEBIT;CODE_CREDIT;TYPE_DEBIT;TYPE_CREDIT;COUNT_DEBIT;COUNT_CREDIT;DEBIT_CHANGE;CREDIT_CHANGE;CUR_FAMILY;FAMILY_EXP;FAMILY_INC;CHECK_CORR;LOAN_SUM;LOAN_CUR;EXP_ITEM_CODE.
- D-236 (the PM; amended by D-244 and D-246). The ID column: a record imported from Excel keeps its Excel ID; every other record (made in the app, the opening balances, the captured PL_family entries) gets `app-<record id>`. An account or category without an import code is written with a computed code `APP_<id>`, not stored. D4b recognises both.
- D-237 (the PM). Columns 16 to 32 (CODE_ITEM to EXP_ITEM_CODE) are computed by the app from columns 1 to 15 and the accounts' and categories' data, by Excel's rules; D4a's parity run is the acceptance.
- D-238 (the PM). An export imported back into the same account changes nothing (0 created, 0 updated, 0 deleted). D4a proves it on the rehearsal for whole-period exports of Excel rows; partial periods and `app-` rows are D4b's.
- D-239 (the PM). A user exports only his own records: his personal ones, and the family records where he is the paying member (Family "да", FAMILY_EXP or FAMILY_INC his side, as in his Excel). Other members' records, the partner's family records that move his FAMILY_DEBT included, are never exported.
- D-240 (the PM). The format is the Excel files': UTF-8 with a byte order mark, `;` between values, CRLF, dates as dd.MM.yyyy, a point before decimals, no thousands separators, no exponent, values as stored (no rounding), currency codes as stored (no conversion), a value quoted only when it holds `;`, `"` or a line break (its quotes doubled), Family "да" or empty, empty or 0 per column exactly as Excel writes it. Rows in the order: Excel IDs ascending, then the `app-` rows by date and id.
- D-241 (the PM). The period is the whole period (the default) or from one date to another, both included, by the record's date as the user sees it. The file's name is `Transactions_<from yyyy-MM-dd>_<to yyyy-MM-dd>.csv`; for the whole period the first and last record's dates.
- D-242 (the PM; its effects as D-249 says). The self-check: for every account and currency, the balance at the end less the balance the day before the start is what the exported rows move, plus what other members' records move, plus what the records skipped as no single row move. Used in tests and in the production suite's export checks.
- D-243 (the PM). The interface is a page of its own, `/export`, linked next to `/import`. No Flyway migration in D4a; if one seems necessary, the agent stops and asks.
- D-244 (the agent, D4a). A family record made in the app is `app-f<record id>` and a personal entry `app-<entry id>`, since entries and records are numbered by two sequences and `app-<id>` alone would name two rows; the captured PL_family lumps are family records, so `app-f<id>`.
- D-245 (the agent, D4a). Where the ledger can't tell two forms of a row apart, the export writes the form whose hash is the one the import stored for the row: the content hash (D-139), D-151's upload hash or D-181's side hash, recomputed by the import's own functions. The forms: the sides as stored or turned with the signs; in two currencies, Sum on the credit side with CUR_DEBIT and SUM_DEBIT, on the debit side with CUR_CREDIT and SUM_CREDIT, or both sides named; the Family flag on a row without an item (D-72); Comment empty or the Counteragent when the import kept no note; LOAN_CA empty or "Unassigned" (the import's name for none); a text the ledger doesn't keep empty or "-". A row with no stored hash, or none that fits, is written in the first form: the sides as stored (a settlement's so that Sum is above 0), Sum on the credit side, no Family flag on a personal entry, the note or else the Counteragent as Comment, the counterparty's name as LOAN_CA, empty for a text not kept.
- D-246 (the agent, D4a). D-236's `APP_<id>` has nothing to apply to: every account and category has a code (NOT NULL, asked for by every form), so the export writes the stored code, also where the import fixed a typo of the workbook's (COMMISSION and GEMEENTE_FUND for COMMISION and GEMENTE_FUND, `Workbook.CODE_ALIASES`); D4b reads both spellings.
- D-247 (the agent, D4a). The one-row forms: a personal entry of two accounts in one currency, or of two accounts with FX_EXCHANGE between them (rule 9); a family expense or income the member paid or received, UNALLOCATED against his paying account (or "Payments without a specified account"), the record's amount on UNALLOCATED and his paying side on the account, Family "да", his payee and note; a settlement with his side, FAMILY_DEBT against his side's account; several settlements of one currency that one row of his matched (D-178's second tier) as that row, their sums; a conversion's two legs (D-71) as one row with FAMILY_DEBT on both sides, the leg he pays on the debit side; his share of a record of his own Excel rows another member paid (D-73, and D-83's lumps), FAMILY_DEBT against UNALLOCATED. His debt to a family budget is named by his ledger's FAMILY_DEBT account (the workbook's), else by the budget's debt account.
- D-248 (the agent, D4a). Skipped as no single row, counted by kind: an entry of another shape (by its kind: OPENING_BALANCE, SHARED_EXPENSE, MANUAL and the like); a member's side matched to several rows of his (D-198, SETTLEMENT_GROUP); a record of his own rows with neither his payment, his side nor his share (FAMILY_RECORD); an entry the family budget posted for no record (a claim's opening balance FAMILY_OPENING, a return's FAMILY_CORRECTION); a family budget's entry while the switch is off (FAMILY_SWITCHED_OFF). Deleted records aren't exported.
- D-249 (the agent, D4a). D-242's effects are the ledger's own postings, as rule 4 displays them, by entry: an exported row's are its entries' (the personal entry; for a family record, his share, payment or settlement side); the other members' records' are his entries for records he doesn't export (his shares of them); the skipped records' are the entries of D-248's kinds. The file's computed columns are Excel's view, which differs from these by D-12's split on family rows (SHARES): that is D4b's row level. The summary gives per account and currency the balance the day before the period, at its end, and the three sums.
- D-250 (the agent, D4a). The API: GET `/api/export/summary` and GET `/api/export/transactions` (`text/csv`, an attachment), both with `from` and `to` or neither; one without the other, or `from` after `to`, answers 400. An empty whole period is `Transactions.csv`. The production suite's read-only export checks are a step (`e2e/export-steps.ts`) in the smoke test (the demo) and in family F8 (A and B in one budget), which write their own data as before, since every spec deletes its data at both ends and a spec of its own would only see empty ledgers; `05-export` is the page's spec, read only in production.
- D-251 (the agent, D4a). D4a's rehearsal restored `finance-2026-10-24T1434Z.dump`, the latest the daily pull had copied: `finance-2026-10-25T0453Z.dump` (D3e's after-dump) wasn't on the laptop, and the server wasn't to be touched. Its counts are those of `verify D3e` (entries 42124, family records 12358, shares 23460, links 35607, journal 12370, categories 95, counterparties 1410).
- D-252 (the agent, D4a; confirmed by the PM in D4b). The parity's differences that the ledger can't reproduce are listed with their cause, not fixed: each is information the import doesn't keep (Counteragent and Comment of a row on FAMILY_DEBT, an amount the import rounded, the workbook's typo in a code, an account's or counterparty's other spelling in a row, a value's spaces, a LOAN_CA the import ignored, the date of a member's settlement row, D-188), and keeping it would need a migration (D-243).

Added in D4b (2026-10-26)
- D-253 (the PM, D4a's deploy). D4a's checklist ran with step 1 skipped, as `main` and `feature/family-budget` were already at M and `run`'s gate 1.5 checks CI on M; steps 2 and 3 ran as one read-only block; the partner's own export wasn't checked by hand, since the rehearsal (D4a's 4c and D-242) and the F8 spec's export step cover it.
- D-254 (the PM; supersedes D-230). No order column: lists stay sorted as they are (accounts by code, categories by name). Names keep the rule of before: a personal account's and category's name is written from the file at every import, now from the rows' Debit, Credit and Exp. Item; a family category keeps its first name. Renames are listed in the dry run.
- D-255 (the PM). FAMILY_DEBT is reconciled together with UNALLOCATED and RESERVE, per currency. The per-account FAMILY_DEBT check and OWNER_HALF (D-169) are dropped: they compared the owner's Excel copy of the partner's figures (PL_family) with the partner's records, and those records are checked against the partner's own file at his import.
- D-256 (the PM; D4a's proposals 5.4 and 5.5). The check compares movements (the balance at a month-end less the balance the day before the file's first row date), so no opening row is needed and D-83's lumps cancel out in the pooled sum; `app-` and `app-f` rows are matched to the ledger by id; a row is "missing" only within the file's period (its first to its last row date, at most "Import up to"); a member's sides and a personal entry are compared by what the ledger holds (D-219 extended, the entry's kind ignored), which removes D4a's 528 rewrites of the partner's sides and the owner's conflict of row 2839.
- D-257 (the PM). Before the check, next to the row count (D-221), the page shows the file's highest ID and its last row date.
- D-258 (the PM). If under D-255 the owner's month-ends no longer depend on the partner's records, D-175's waiting points and the owner's closing dry run in step 12 go away: step 12 becomes the owner, then the partner. Shown in D4b's rehearsal (each participant's entries for the other's records move the pooled sum by exactly 0 per currency and no other account; the owner's check passes before the partner's import as after it), so applied.
- D-259 (the PM). `app-` and `app-f` rows are never written by the import: one unchanged is nothing to do; one changed, an unknown id, or another ledger's id is listed and not applied. D-243 still holds: no migration.
- D-260 (the agent, D4b). The partner's family rows for a place without an account (D-140, D-151) stay: "the four other inputs" removed are the accounts, categories and daily files; the page has one file of the user's own, and the partner's upload only for such a place, as before. The browser's filter takes UNALLOCATED's name from the user's own file (a row whose CODE_DEBIT or CODE_CREDIT is UNALLOCATED) and adds each row's CODE_ITEM and TYPE where his file has D-235's columns, as the owner's file needn't name every item of the partner's; D-151's hash leaves them out, so the rows are the same rows.
- D-261 (the agent, D4b). The names of one code are compared ignoring case, as the journal spells an account in any case ("Moneycard V-Pay" and "…V-PAY"); the name written is the app's when it is one of the file's spellings, else the spelling most rows use, the first by ID on a tie. On production's data the owner's MONEYCARD_DEBIT takes his rows' spelling at his next commit (listed as a rename).
- D-262 (the agent, D4b). A name that two codes of the file share (accounts in any case, categories exactly) refuses the file too, as rows name their accounts and categories by name (D-86's uniqueness of the accounts file, carried over).
- D-263 (the agent, D4b). The row level (D-231 a): a computed number is the typed columns' within a billionth of its size, as the CSV keeps 10 significant digits of a number, a typed Sum's too, while Excel computes from the whole one (23 of the owner's rows, 4 of the partner's); a typed amount of two or more decimals that the CSV shows rounded, the computed columns keeping the whole value (the partner's rows 836, 1083, 1084, 2281 and 4127, all under half a cent), is explained as SHOWN_ROUNDED, and that difference goes into the month-ends; what the import builds against the row's columns, less its drift, fails above 0.0001.
- D-264 (the agent, D4b). No tail (D-69): every month-end from the file's first row date up to the cutoff (its last row date, at most "Import up to") is compared, and the cutoff itself; the file is both sides.
- D-265 (the agent, D4b). NO_ROW: what the ledger's entries move that the export writes no row for and no row of the file names (D-248: the opening balances, a claim's opening balance, a return's correction, an entry of another shape, and a side of several rows of his, D-198) is explained; it replaces D-180's CLAIM_OPENING. A side of several rows is the file's when any of those rows is in it.
- D-266 (the agent, D4b). An `app-` ID of another user's entry or record gets the same answer as an ID that names nothing (rule 11): "names no entry or family record of yours".
- D-267 (the agent, D4b). `family_import_cutoff` (V15) is neither read nor written any more (D-258); the table and its rows stay (no migration, D-243).
- D-268 (the agent, D4b). The records of D-83's lumps (`xls:<owner>:PL:…`) are never missing: no row of a file stands for them (D-232).
- D-269 (the agent, D4b). Excel gives a family row without an item (Exp. Item "-") no family part: FAMILY_EXP and FAMILY_INC are 0 (the partner's rows 7766 and 8097). `ExcelRow` (D-237) had it otherwise and is fixed, for the export of D-72's form too.
- D-270 (the agent, D4b). The type check of D-229 compares the workbook's types: rule 4 makes LIABILITIES a LIABILITY or an EQUITY account (OPENING_BALANCE, FX_EXCHANGE, an equity of the user's), so only ASSET against LIABILITIES is another type.
- D-271 (the agent, D4b). A listed `app-` row (D-259) counts among the conflicts in the totals (source APP). The commit's semantics stay: a failing check doesn't hold a commit back (step 12's STOP rules decide), a row error does.
- D-272 (the agent, D4b). A member's side is what the ledger holds when his own entry for the record has a line on the row's account in its currency, of its amount (either sign), with its counterparty, and for a payment the payee and note the row gives: then it is unchanged, written nothing, its stored hash left as it was (D-256).

Added in OPS-3 (2026-10-26; decided by the PM, as OPS-3's task states them)
- D-273 (the PM, D4b's deploy). D4b's checklist ran with step 1 skipped, as `main` and `feature/family-budget` were already at M and `run`'s gate 1.5 checks CI on M; with no separate pre-flight block and no checkpoint before `run`: `verify D4a`, then `run`, whose own pre-flight refuses on any mismatch that matters; without the state block read again after the rollback acceptance, since `rollback.sh` prints "Nothing changed"; and without D-163's first sign-in, a one-time check of step 10.3.
- D-274 (the PM). The order: OPS-3 now; October's step 12 in early November, with the rehearsal from production's latest dump (D-220), a task of its own; changes to the import (D4c: Commit unavailable while the check fails, the owner's paying side compared) only after it.
- D-275 (the PM). Hetzner's Backups on the server `auth-1`: 7 daily backups of the whole server, for about 20% of its price. The PM switches them on in the Hetzner Console, as a checklist step that says what to click and what to paste back. The runbook says what a backup holds (the whole disk, taken while the server runs, so its databases are crash-consistent; the nightly dumps stay the way to restore a database; a Hetzner backup is for a lost or broken server), its window and whether it can overlap the dumps (02:30 UTC) or the reboot window (D-125), and how to restore a server from one, from Hetzner's own documentation, cited, with what it couldn't confirm.
- D-276 (the PM). An external monitor: a scheduled GitHub Actions workflow in this repository, every 15 minutes (best effort), and `workflow_dispatch`. Checks, each with a short timeout and one retry: the app's page answers 200 with `<div id="root">`; `/api/me` without a session answers 401; the realm's discovery document answers 200 with its issuer; both certificates valid for 14 more days. A failure fails the run and GitHub emails the owner (how that is routed and what the owner must have set, written down); an input that fails on purpose, for one check after the deploy that the email arrives; no secret, no server access, nothing written. The monitor's runs never block or change `deploy.sh`'s gate, also at OPS-3's own deploy, whose `run` is `9af167d`'s. GitHub's 60 days without activity in a public repository, and how to notice and enable it again, written down.
- D-277 (the PM). Logs kept 14 days. Every container of both stacks logs to the journal; the journal keeps 14 days (`MaxRetentionSec=14day`, files rotated daily so that nothing older than 15 days stays, a size cap as a safety limit today's volume can't reach, measured). Everything else on the host that keeps addresses or user data (rsyslog's files, fail2ban's log and database, the deploy run folders, Keycloak's events) within 14 days or listed with its own period. The privacy policy states every period as a true upper bound, the copies in backups included. Each recreation of a container is a planned checklist step after fresh backups and restore tests of both databases, in an order that keeps each outage short, never an implicit `docker compose up`; `deploy.sh`'s guard on the postgres service stays meaningful. Everything that reads container logs keeps working, tested.
- D-278 (the PM). No captcha now. The checklist has a read-only look at realm `myapps` (users in all, created in the last 30 days, with an email not verified; `REGISTER` events in the last 30 days); if it shows anything that looks like bots, STOP: the captcha becomes a stage of its own.
- D-279 (the PM). D-216 built: `pg-backup` prints the ready restore line for its own dump (the auth server's repository, installed by its `deploy/backup/install.sh`).
- D-280 (the agent, OPS-3). The gate (`deploy.sh` 1.5) counts the check runs of the runs of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and of no other workflow: GitHub's list of that workflow's runs of the commit (`actions/workflows/ci.yml/runs?head_sha=`), then each run's check suite (`check-suites/<id>/check-runs`), joined and judged as before. A list longer than its page, or a run of another file, refuses. The smallest change that keeps the gate's meaning (every check run of CI succeeded) while the monitor's runs, which attach to `main`'s head every 15 minutes, would otherwise fail it, keep it pending, or push the commit's check runs past one page of 100. "CI on Ubuntu 26.04" no longer counts either; D-44's rule for it stays.
- D-281 (the agent, OPS-3). `deploy.sh`'s preflight (1.4) compares the compose file's extension blocks (`x-…`) too: the postgres service merges `x-logging` in, so a change there is a change of the postgres service, which stays manual. `9af167d`'s guard, which runs OPS-3's deploy, compared the postgres block's own text and lets OPS-3's change through.
- D-282 (the agent, OPS-3). The driver is `journald` with the tag `{{.Name}}` (the journal's `SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER`: `journalctl -t finance-tracker-api`), in both stacks' `x-logging`. The journal's settings: `/etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/60-retention.conf` with `Storage=persistent`, `MaxRetentionSec=14day`, `MaxFileSec=1day`, `SystemMaxUse=1G`. The volume can't be measured from the laptop (production is read only for the agent): the checklist's inventory measures each container's lines a day from its current `json-file` logs, and 15 days of them must stay under half the cap (under 30 000 lines a day at 1 KB an entry), else STOP.
- D-283 (the agent, OPS-3; a deviation from OPS-3's task, which said to fast-forward `main` and push both branches). OPS-3 pushes `feature/family-budget` only, and fast-forwards the laptop's `main` without pushing it; the checklist's step 6 pushes `main` shortly before `run`. GitHub runs a schedule only from the default branch, so the monitor's runs start on M then, and `9af167d`'s gate, which counts every check run of M, sees only the few of the next hours (more than 90 would push past its page of 100 within about 22 hours, permanently for M), each of which must have succeeded; one that failed is re-run once.
- D-284 (the agent, OPS-3). rsyslog's files (a copy of the journal: `ForwardToSyslog=yes` is the rsyslog package's setting), `fail2ban.log`, `btmp` and `wtmp` rotate daily with 13 old files and `maxage 14`, without `notifempty` or `minsize`, so that none holds anything older than 14 days and logrotate's hour of random delay; the packages' files are kept in `/root/ops3-before/`. rsyslog stays (fail2ban's sshd jail reads the journal, Ubuntu 26.04's default, which the inventory checks).
- D-285 (the agent, OPS-3). The copies of the before-dumps in the deploy run folders (D-43), whole databases kept since OPS-2, are deleted 14 days after their run by `finance-deploy-dumps.timer` (daily at 05:15 UTC); the run folders' other files hold no user data and stay, as `finish`, `adopt` and `rollback.sh` read them. `rollback.sh` already says when a copy is gone.
- D-286 (the agent, OPS-3). The auth server's `sync.sh --copy-only` copies without starting or recreating anything; the recreations are explicit steps (`docker compose up -d --force-recreate --no-deps SERVICE`) after `run` and after fresh dumps and restore tests of both databases, in this order: the finance `postgres` (about 5 to 15 s of `/api` errors), Keycloak stopped then the auth `postgres` and Keycloak (about 30 to 90 s without sign-in), Caddy (about 2 to 5 s without HTTPS). The api and web take the driver from `run`, as at every deploy.
- D-287 (the agent, OPS-3). Kept with their own periods, not changed: Keycloak's events (30 and 90 days; D-278 reads 30 days of them), fail2ban's database (a day, the package's default), the last login per account, and the one-off copies of 2026-09-27 in `/root` (`keycloak-2026-09-27.sql`, `auth-config-2026-09-27/`), which the PM may delete.
- D-288 (the agent, OPS-3). The monitor: `.github/workflows/monitor.yml` at minutes 7, 22, 37 and 52 (away from the hour's peak), one job "Monitor", read-only token, its checks in `.github/scripts/monitor.sh` (curl and openssl with `-verify_hostname`; 10 s to connect, 20 s in all, one retry after 10 s; `--fail` for the email's test), tested by `.github/scripts/tests/monitor-test.sh` in CI's "Deploy scripts" job.
- D-289 (the agent, OPS-3). The privacy policy's periods: logs at most 15 days on the server, 23 with Hetzner's backups (a backup lives at most about 8 days); deleted data 23 days on the server and in its backups, 61 on the owner's computer (60 days and its next daily run); Keycloak's events 91 and 151 days, their copies in the dumps included; "Last updated: 26 October 2026". Logs written before the host's settings age out within 15 days of them.

From and To stay; editing one makes the period custom, with no button pressed. The chart's and the table's columns stay calendar months, and a partial first or last month says so ("from 16 Sep"). "Balances on" defaults as before; old bookmarks keep working.

- D-302 (the PM, UX-1). A "Show" control for income and expenses: "All" (the default), "Personal" (the user's entries that no family record posted, whatever their category), "Family: my records" (the user's shares, link type SHARE, of records whose payer, or receiver for an income, is the user's own member) and "Family: others' records" (the user's shares of records another member paid or received, with or without an account). The amounts are the user's own shares as the ledger holds them, so Personal and both family scopes add up to All per category, month and currency, exactly in each currency and within 2 minor units in the base currency; the exchange results appear under All only. An entry is classified by its SHARE link and that record's payer, never by its category, a detached link by its record. With more than one family budget, the family scopes get a budget selector. `/api/reports/cash-flow` takes `scope` (`ALL`, `PERSONAL`, `FAMILY_MINE`, `FAMILY_OTHERS`) and an optional `familyLedgerId` in both shapes; another user's, a personal or an unknown budget is 404, a family scope with the switch off 400. In the URL `scope=personal|family-mine|family-others` and `family=<id>`. The scope affects the period's totals, the chart and the table only, sits in the "Income and expenses" section, and a family scope links "Whole family budget →" to that budget's report of the same period.

It applies to every day date shown (`formatDate` without month-only options, the date part of `formatInstant`) and typed: one `DateInput` replaces all 19 `<input type="date">` (a text field in the format with its pattern as placeholder, one- or two-digit days and months, the separators `.`, `/` and `-`, a calendar button opening the browser's own picker, an ISO value, an error under the field for text that isn't a date, the native `min` and `max`); the browser's way types in its locale's numeric order. Equal to what his row maps to: unchanged. Not equal, his row as last imported: "changed in the app, the file unchanged", left alone, listed by Excel ID, its balance effect explained as APP_EDITED (D-223). - D-395 (the PM, UX-5). The comparison ranges, pure functions in `dashboard.ts` from the period's From and To. The previous period, first rule that matches: whole months (From the 1st, To a month's last day), k of them: the k months before; whole weeks (From a Monday, To a Sunday), k of them: the k weeks before; a rolling span of k months (From the day after To's date k months back, clamped to that month's end, as "Past month" and "Past year"): the k-month span by the same rule ending the day before From; otherwise as many days, ending the day before From. The year earlier: for whole months the same months a year back (February 2029 against all of February 2028); for whole weeks 52 weeks back (364 days); otherwise the same dates a year back, 29 February as 28 February; none where it would overlap the period (a period longer than a year); where it equals the previous period (any year-long period), one comparison only, headed "Previous year". Labels: "Oct 2026", "Q3 2026", "2025" for whole months, quarters and years, else the dates through `Intl.DateTimeFormat`'s `formatRange`; the table's header puts the role above the label: "Year earlier", "Previous period" or "Previous year". The worked example on Monday 2026-11-09 is a test (`compare.test.ts`).
- D-396 (the PM, UX-5). A running period (From ≤ today < To, today being `useToday()`'s) is compared to date: income and expenses cover From to today (the totals, the chart and the table), the heading gives those dates and "to date"; the period buttons and To are unchanged. Each comparison ends at today moved by its own shift (k months clamped to the month's end, 7k days, the span's length in days, a year, 364 days), within its range. So "Result for the period" (D-367), which ends at Balances on, today by default, then matches by default. Entries dated after today leave the section until their day.
- D-397 (the PM, UX-5). Four more presets: "This week" and "Previous week" (Monday to Sunday), "This quarter" and "Previous quarter" (calendar quarters), `period=this-week|previous-week|this-quarter|previous-quarter`. A custom range is compared by D-395's rules too.
- D-398 (the PM, UX-5). The change in %: (period − comparison) / |comparison| × 100 with big.js, rounded half up to one decimal, always signed ("+12.3%", "−4.0%", "0.0%"); "—" when the comparison has nothing or 0; a period with nothing against a comparison with something is −100.0%; with a figure that lacks a rate the % cell is empty and the amount says "rate missing". Coloured by its effect on the net (income, Total income and Net up good; expenses and Total expenses up bad; 0 uncoloured), the sign kept so colour is never the only cue; amounts keep their colours. D-400's line lists the previous period first, then the year earlier (or the one "Previous year"), as D-400's example does, and leaves out a comparison whose % is "—" or empty (nothing to compare with, or a missing rate); with none left, no line. The line is a second `dd` of the figure, so its first stays the amount.
- D-405 (the agent, UX-5). D-399's pairing counts calendar months: the year earlier is drawn when it covers as many months as the section, which 52 weeks back may not (13 weeks from Monday 2 August to Sunday 31 October 2027, 3 months, against 3 August to 1 November 2026, 4), and then isn't drawn; one "Previous year" (D-395) counts as the year earlier. With only one comparison drawable, no toggle; `compare=previous` without a drawable previous period draws the year earlier.
- D-406 (the agent, UX-5). The section shows once the period and every comparison have answered or failed, so no column arrives late; while a new choice loads, the figures stay dimmed (`stale`). A comparison that failed leaves its two columns out and shows "Year earlier (Oct 2025): " and the error above the totals; the period and the other comparison stay. "No income or expenses" shows only when the period and every comparison are empty; a period without any but a comparison with some shows the table, "—" in the period.
- D-407 (the agent, UX-5; finding). The exchange-rate lines are shown where any column has a figure other than 0, and then each column shows its own, a 0.00 as €0.00: on production's dump, October 2025 and September 2026 answered revaluation 0.00, which the first build showed as "—" since a range whose results are all 0 hides its lines.

The "Show" control chooses the family budget: All, Personal, then with two or more family budgets "All family budgets", then each family budget by its name, sorted by name; with one budget, its button alone. Once a family budget or "All family budgets" is chosen, a second control below it, "Records": "All records" (the default), "My records", "Others’ records", with D-302's meanings for the last two. The budget's `<select>` goes. "Whole family budget →" stays as now: the chosen budget's, or one link per budget for all of them. URL: `scope=family|family-mine|family-others`, and `family=<id>` for one budget, none for all of them; with one budget, no `family` means that budget, so an old link with `scope=family-mine` still opens it pressed. A budget's button sets `family`, "All family budgets" removes it, "Records" keeps it. Without a membership, or with the switch off, the control isn't shown, as before. Why in the backend: in the base currency each figure is rounded once (D-49), so the browser can't add two scopes' figures into a third.
