Something against 0 stays "—", and 0 against something stays −100.0%, as before. One rule in `dashboard.ts`, shared by the table, the totals and the chart's tooltip.
- D-441 (the owner, UX-7). In the Entries list a row names its family budget once, twice at most; the owner's 2026-10-31 rows named it four or five times.
- D-442 (the PM, UX-7). The family line under the category names the budget once: the kind as before, then UX-6's `SharedMark` icon and the budget's name ("Family share · <icon> Family Budget"); without a category, the icon and the name. In this list, category names in the Category and Accounts columns lose `categoryLabel`'s " · <budget>". The row's own debt account reads "Debt to family budget", without ": <budget>": the system account whose code is `FAMILY_DEBT_<the row's family.ledgerId>`, or that code with `_2`, `_3`…, as `CrossLedgerWriter.debtAccount` names it; every other account keeps its name. So the owner's 2026-10-31 rows read "Продукты" with "Family share · <icon> Family Budget" under it, Accounts "Debt to family budget → Продукты"; the same in the 40rem layout (D-351). The filters, the entry's page and the other pages keep their labels: in a `<select>` the suffix tells two budgets' categories apart.
- D-443 (the owner, UX-7, answering the agent's question). D-430 for a running period: the balances, net worth, loans and the family settlement are as of the earlier of To and today (`dashboard.balancesDay`), and "Result for the period" runs from From to that day (`resultRange`), so the equity lines still add up (D-367, D-369); the Balances card's title names that day (D-431). Why: the backend converts a balance at the rate on its `asOf`, and an ECB rate counts only up to 7 days after its date (D-49, `RateBook.ECB_DAYS`), so as of To (30 November on 10 November) every foreign-currency figure of the headline, the Balances card and open loans would read "rate missing" in the base currency for most of each running month, and always for This quarter, This year and Last 3 months. A running period therefore leaves out entries dated after today, which D-430's last sentence had accepted. A period that has ended is as of its To; an old link's `asOf` is still ignored, and there is no "Balances on".

The Entries page enables its Custom while its range is custom, which a user can't tell apart. Choosing a preset after a custom range drops `from` and `to`, as its button did, and an old `asOf` with them.
- D-445 (the agent, UX-7). D-434 in detail (`Header.tsx`): `Header` holds which menu is open (one at a time) and closes it on any change of path or query; `Disclosure` closes on Escape anywhere in the document (focus back to its button), on a `mousedown` outside its own element, and on a click on a link or button inside its panel. The panel element is always there (`hidden` while closed, so `aria-controls` points at it), and its items are rendered only while it is open: a finding of the tests, where a closed menu's "Family budgets" list matched a page's own label ("Family budget"). The family group is a list labelled by its "Family budgets" line (a paragraph, not a heading, so the page's headings stay as they were); "New family budget…" is in it with no budget too. "Inside a family budget" is a path `/family/<id>…` other than `/family/new`, only with family budgets on. At 375 px the header doesn't wrap (`flex-wrap: nowrap`); the user's button shrinks first, its name cut by `text-overflow: ellipsis`; the right-hand panel is aligned to its button's right edge, the left one to its left edge, each at most the viewport less 2rem wide.

D-440 keeps D-398's formula, the change divided by the comparison's size: two figures of the same sign give a percentage as before (a refund-heavy category that went from −100 to −150 reads −50.0%); 0 against a negative comparison reads +100.0%, D-440's "−100.0%" being the usual case of a positive one. `changeOf(now, before, net)`; the table and the totals pass `net` for the Net line, the chart's tooltip has no Net line.

- D-453 (the owner, UX-8). The New entry page finds the user's earlier entries like the one being typed. As Payee, Paid from, Amount, Category or Memo are filled in, a list of matching saved entries appears under the form after a short pause. Every filled field must match: the more are filled, the shorter the list. Each listed entry has a Copy button that fills the new entry from it (D-461).
- D-454 (the owner, UX-8). Text fields match loosely (D-458). Amount matches by its characters, or when the two amounts differ by less than 1 in the form's currency (D-459).
- D-455 (the owner, UX-8). "Save and add another" keeps every field as it was saved, so the next entry needs only what differs (D-462).
- D-456 (the PM, UX-8). Where, and what counts as a criterion. Only on a new entry (`/entries/new`), never on an entry's own page. On the Expense and Income tabs, whose fields these are; Income's Payer and Received into are its Payee and Paid from. Transfer, Loan, Currency exchange and Advanced show no list (a later stage if the owner asks). The criteria are the fields as the form shows them. With "Family expense" or "Family income" on, Category is the family category and Memo the private note. A field the form hides is no criterion (a family income has no Payer). A category the form put in by itself from the payee's last category (`withPayee`) is no criterion until the user picks one; otherwise it would hide the family expenses at that payee, whose category is the record's. What Copy puts in starts no new search: the list stays as it is until the user changes a field.
- D-457 (the PM, UX-8). Which saved entries the list holds. Expense: the user's personal expenses, refunds included, and his own payments of family expenses. A payment counts when its entry is linked to the record as his PAYMENT, the link isn't detached, the record isn't deleted, he is an ACTIVE member and family budgets are on. About three in four of the owner's rows since 2025 are family expenses he paid; without them the list misses most of his history. Income: his personal incomes, and his receipts of family incomes on the same terms. Left out: family shares, settlements, opening balances and corrections, and records another member paid, which the form can't make. Old shared expenses (SHARED_EXPENSE) are left out while family budgets are on (H4: a new entry can't be one); with them off they count as expenses. Only the user's personal ledger, as every personal endpoint.
- D-458 (the PM, UX-8). Loose text matching, the same for Payee and Memo. Case doesn't count, in Latin or Cyrillic; ё and е are the same letter, and so are Ё and Е. The typed text is split into words at spaces; each word must appear somewhere in the field, in any order, also inside a word: "alb" and "heijn alb" find "Albert Heijn", "прод" finds "Продукты". `%`, `_` and `\` are plain characters. A text field counts from 2 characters, trimmed; with fewer it is no criterion. Payee is the entry's payee (a family payment's is the payer's own, D-81). Memo is the entry's memo (a family payment's is the private note). Neither looks at the other field or at the family comment. Typos aren't forgiven: that would need PostgreSQL's `pg_trgm`, an extension in production and a migration, a later stage if the owner asks. PostgreSQL folds case by the database's locale; the stage's check shows that production's does.
- D-459 (the PM, UX-8). Amount. The criterion is the typed amount as `parseAmount` reads it, without its sign; text it can't read is no criterion. An entry's amounts are its lines that aren't on an EQUITY account, each as a magnitude in its own currency: for an expense its paying account's line; for a family payment its account's line and its line on the family budget's debt account, which is in the record's currency. An entry matches when one of its amounts, written with its currency's decimals (the minor unit, HALF_UP, a dot, no grouping: `1212.50` EUR, `1001` JPY), contains the criterion as `parseAmount` writes it, in any currency ("12.5" finds 1212.50, "1001" finds 1001 JPY and 10010.00 EUR); or is in the form's Currency and differs from the criterion by less than 1 (with 100 EUR, 99.01 and 100.99 match; 99.00 and 101.00 don't, unless by their characters).
- D-460 (the PM, UX-8). The list: under the form's buttons, headed "Similar entries" with how many match ("Similar entries · 23"). It asks 400 ms after the last change of a criterion, and drops an answer to an older question; without a criterion there is no list and no request. Newest first, 10 at a time, "Show 10 more" while there are more. Each row shows the date in the user's format, the payee, the account; the category, a family row as the Entries list's family line (UX-6's `SharedMark` and the budget's name); the amount and currency, a refund as the Entries list shows one; the memo, muted; and Copy, its accessible name naming the row ("Copy the entry of 03.11.2026, Albert Heijn, €11.35"). At 375 px each row is a block of lines, as in the Entries list (D-351); nothing scrolls sideways. A failed request shows one muted line, "Couldn't look for similar entries.", and never blocks the form. After a save it asks again. The count is announced politely (`aria-live`), not each row.
- D-461 (the PM, UX-8). Copy keeps the form's date. It keeps the form's amount and currency when Amount holds a valid amount; everything else comes from the row: tab, payee, account, category, memo, refund. A personal row is copied as `formFromEntry` opens it, with Family expense or income off; one whose postings don't fit its tab opens in Advanced, as on its own page: its postings as they are, with that page's notice, and nothing kept but the date. A family row turns Family expense (or income) on: its budget, family category, comment and refund from the record (`GET /api/family-ledgers/{id}/records/{recordId}`), with the record's amount and currency unless the form's are kept; the payment's account (`yourPayment.accountId`; "Specify later" leaves the form's) and payee, and the entry's memo as the private note; the split as a new record's: ONE_MEMBER, PERCENT and AMOUNT as stored, EQUAL becomes RULE if the budget's rule is EQUAL now, else PERCENT with equal shares (`basisPoints.equalShares`) among the members who share on the form's date; a split by amounts that no longer adds up shows the split editor's own message; a paying side in another currency isn't copied (D-89). An account or a category archived since isn't copied: the field stays empty, with a line under it saying why. After a copy, a status line says "Copied from the entry of <date>. Check the date and the amount.", and focus moves to Amount.
- D-462 (the PM, UX-8). Save and add another: every field stays as saved, the tab too. The status reads "Saved. The form keeps its values: change what differs." Focus moves to Amount, where the tab has one, its text selected. Saving again with nothing changed since asks "Save the same entry again?" (`confirm`, as Delete asks), from either button. A payee or person the save created is reused by the next save on both paths (`EntryEditor.saveFamily` created a new payee without adding it to `created`, and `EntryForms.saveFamily` looked it up in a ledger that didn't have it yet, so a kept new payee would have been created twice). The list asks again (D-460). "Save" still goes back to the list.
- D-463 (the PM, UX-8). `GET /api/entries/matches`, read only, in the personal ledger's scope: `type` (`EXPENSE` or `INCOME`, required); `payee`, `memo`, each at most 100 characters; `accountId` (an entry with a line on that account); `categoryId` (the entry's category as below); `amount`, a plain decimal at or above 0 (D-459), with `currency` (ISO 4217; without it only the characters count); `page`, and `size`, 10 by default and at most 50. At least one criterion, else 400. The answer is a page with `totalElements`, newest first (date, then id, descending); each item holds the entry as `GET /api/entries` gives it, and what its row shows: `amount` and `currency` (a personal entry's line on its account, or a family row's record's amount and currency), `categoryId` (the categorized line's, or the record's) and `refund`. `GET /api/entries` and its `q` stay as they are. If a search on production's dump takes more than 300 ms in the api, stop and ask.
- D-464 (the PM, UX-8; two findings of UX-7's review, which the owner may drop). The Entries page at 375 px doesn't scroll sideways on the owner's names (D-451): `.filters select { max-width: 100% }` is already there, so find what still widens the page and fix that. Under 40rem the user's button shows a person icon and the chevron in place of the cut name: the icon an inline SVG drawn for the app, `currentColor`; the button's accessible name and `title` the whole name, and the panel's first line the name, muted, not a link. From 40rem as now.
- D-465 (the agent, UX-8). The answer's item nests the entry: `{entry, amount, currency, categoryId, refund}` (`EntryMatch`, `EntryMatchPage`), so that `entry` is exactly `GET /api/entries`' item. `EntryMatchService` is its own service in `ledger/`; it reuses `EntryService.header` and `withPostings`, made package-private for it.
- D-466 (the agent, UX-8). D-457 in SQL: a personal expense (or income) has a posting whose category is of the type (rule 6), no `family_entry_link` of any kind, detached or not, and a kind that isn't one of the family's; while the family switch is on, SHARED_EXPENSE is left out whether or not the user has a family budget. A family row needs the link's member to be the user's own ACTIVE member of that budget (`LedgerAccess.families`). An entry with both an expense and an income line would count under both types; none such was found on production's dump.
- D-467 (the agent, UX-8). D-458 in SQL: `strpos(translate(lower(field), 'ёЁ', 'ее'), translate(lower(word), 'ёЁ', 'ее')) > 0` for each word, so no LIKE pattern and nothing to escape; words split at any run of whitespace. The backend applies the 2-character rule too: a `payee` or `memo` under 2 characters, trimmed, is no criterion, so with nothing else it is a 400.
- D-468 (the agent, UX-8). D-459 and D-463 in detail: `amount` is up to 15 digits with up to 4 decimals (rule 3's NUMERIC(19,4)), else 400; `currency` three capital letters, else 400. The decimals come from Java's `Currency` for every currency, 2 for one it doesn't know, in `round(abs(amount), digits)`; "less than 1" compares the line's stored magnitude. A personal row's `amount` is its first line, by `line_no`, on an account that isn't EQUITY; its `refund` the sign of its categorized line against the type (rule 5).
- D-469 (the agent, UX-8). The list's details D-460 leaves open: a row's amount carries its sign as the Entries list shows a personal one (an expense with a minus, a refund with a plus), and a family row by its record's type and refund, which the Entries list shows unsigned; a row's account is its first line neither EQUITY nor a family budget's debt account; the category is shown by its name, with the family line under it; with no match the list says "No saved entry matches."; the error line uses the app's typographic apostrophe ("Couldn’t look for similar entries."); the heading itself is the polite live region; "Show 10 more" keeps its 10 for a last page of fewer.
- D-470 (the agent, UX-8). Copy's details: an entry of an Advanced kind (MANUAL, OPENING_BALANCE) keeps only the date, like one that doesn't fit its tab, but without the notice, as its own page has none; a family record paid from an account that keeps balances per person (D-80), which the personal form doesn't offer, leaves Paid from empty with a line, as an archived one; a family row's Copy asks for the record and the budget's members at the click, and a failure shows "Couldn’t copy the entry: …" at the top of the form, the form unchanged; what Copy puts in counts as picked, so its category is a criterion of the next search (D-456 exempts only `withPayee`'s); a member who shares the form's date without a stored share gets 0 under PERCENT and AMOUNT; the "Copied from" line, like "Saved…", goes at the next change, a field's line at the next change of that field, and Advanced's notice when the tab changes.
- D-471 (the agent, UX-8). "Nothing changed since" (D-462) is the whole form state equal to what "Save and add another" last saved; "Save" leaves the page, so it sets nothing. The family path's payee: `EntryFormView` passes the typed name, and `EntryEditor.saveFamily` finds it among the counterparties and those the page created, creating it only when neither has it, and adds it to `created`.
- D-472 (the agent, UX-8). D-464's fixes. The Entries page: a filter's `<label>` is a flex item as wide as its content, so `max-width: 100%` on its select resolved against a label that a select as wide as its longest option had already widened; `.filters > label { max-width: 100%; min-width: 0 }` caps the label at the fieldset, and the select at the label. The header: under 40rem `svg.person` is shown and `.user-name` kept as a line of no width (hidden, it left the button shorter than the header's other buttons, and `07-dashboard`'s one-row check failed); the button takes `aria-label` with the whole name (a hidden name names nothing), and the panel's first line, `p.menu-user-name`, is shown only under 40rem. The rules sit at the end of `index.css`, after UX-4's, whose test reads the stylesheet before its first `@media`.
- D-473 (the agent, UX-8). End to end: `08-new-entry` (the specs' pattern is `0[2-8]` now); its personal expenses use "Eating out", since `createTheBudget` merges Groceries into the family budget; with `E2E_FAMILY=on` the family expense is saved from New entry with its Family expense switch, which posts the payment the list finds.
- D-474 (the agent, UX-8; finding). On production's dump `finance-2026-11-10T0231Z.dump` (SHA-256 `52e41290…`, the newest the laptop holds; entries 42743, as UX-7's pre-flight read production), through `scripts/rehearsal-db.sh`, the owner as `testuser`, the api on the host: the similar entries of payee "albert": 700, the first three his family payments at Albert Heijn; payee "albert" and amount "11" in EUR: 45; memo "прод": 1034; amount "17.99" in EUR: 85 (the first 18.95 by D-459's "within 1", then 17.99 by its characters); Paid from "ING Debit Card" with the family category Продукты: 954 (the first three refunds, "+"); Income with memo "перевод от": 0, rightly: no income of either participant has it in its memo, and the owner's ten entries that do are 9 transfers and a loan repaid, which D-457 leaves out. The medians of 5 requests, timed at the client over loopback after one untimed: 95, 167, 76, 191, 83 and 68 ms, all under D-463's 300 ms. Copy of the newest Albert Heijn row: Family expense on, its family category Продукты, the payment's account, its private note, the comment empty, the budget's rule (EQUAL) as the split, the record's 4.76 EUR (Amount was empty) and the form's date. At 375 px New entry with the list, `/entries` (D-464's filters) and the header with the user's menu open are 375 px wide, no sideways scroll. `UX-8.sql` on the dump equals `UX-8.expected`. Figures and screenshots in `data/private/reports/UX-8/`, not in the repository.

- D-475 (the PM, UX-8's deploy). The PM's notes of UX-8's deploy aren't in yet. UX-9 records nothing of it and leaves "Production now" and "Deployed revisions" as they are; the next stage records UX-8's and UX-9's deploys together. UX-9's pre-flight prints the running images in full, as UX-8's did (D-452).
- D-476 (the owner). In the Entries list a family payment shows its record's category, as the record's own page does, instead of "Family payment" alone.
- D-477 (the PM). How a family row's category reads. A row with no categorized line whose family link has a record with a category (the payer's PAYMENT of an expense, a refund's too, and the receipt of an income) shows that category's name in the Category cell, as a share's is shown: not muted. The family line under it then reads as a share's does (D-442): the kind (`entryKindLabel`: "Family payment", "Family income received"), " · ", `SharedMark` with the budget's name. A settlement, a family opening balance and a correction have no category: unchanged. Display only: the entry's amount, its sign and colour (`describeEntry`'s direction), the dashboard, the cash flow, reports, balances, the export and UX-8's similar entries don't change; `describeEntry` doesn't count the record's category as a categorized line. The API: `EntryFamily` gets `recordCategory: { id, name }` for every non-detached link whose record has a category, left out otherwise (additive, as `recordType`); the name is the family category's current one, archived included; the frontend doesn't look it up in the ledger's list, which holds only ACTIVE memberships' categories. At 375 px the cell is as a share's (D-351).
- D-478 (the PM). Entries' Category filter. `GET /api/entries?categoryId=` also finds an entry whose non-detached family link's record has that category, reading links as `families()` does: a row is found by a category exactly when its Category cell shows that category. So the family category Электроника lists the owner's payment (ING Debit Card → Debt to family budget, €10.00) and his share (−€5.00) of the same expense, as the unfiltered list already does; the Categories page's "Show its entries" link follows. The count and paging as now; no other filter changes.
- D-479 (the owner). A member can let the other members of a family budget see the payees of their family payments, in the budget's settings. Each switch, on or off, asks for confirmation first.
- D-480 (the PM). Who decides. Each member decides for their own payees, separately in each budget, whatever their role; nobody switches it for anyone else, an owner included. Off by default, for every member now and every new one. Why: the payee is the payer's own data on their own entry (D-81), and the invite page, the payment form's hints and the privacy policy promise that the others never see it; a switch an owner turned for everyone would show another member's payees without their say. So for the owner to see the other member's payees, the other member switches his on. Only an ACTIVE member with an account has the switch: a member without an account pays from no account and has no payee; LEFT and FORMER members' links are detached.
- D-481 (the PM). What the others see, and where. Only the payee's name on the payer's PAYMENT entry for a record (D-81): an expense, a refund (D-79), an income's receipt; never the account, the account line's counterparty (D-80), the private note, the paying amount or currency (D-88), or anything of a settlement's sides. Read when shown, never copied: switching on shows the payees of past records too; switching off hides them all at once; a payee renamed later shows its new name; a payee removed from the entry shows nothing. Shown to the ACTIVE members of that budget only, while the payer is an ACTIVE member whose switch is on. Where: the budget's Activity list (`RecordTable`, so the Overview's recent records too), after who paid on the line under the category; the record's page, a "Payee" line with "Shown by <payer's display name>." under it. The payer sees their own in both places too, with "Only you see it." or "The other members see it." by their switch. Not shown: the journal, the report, balances, the export, import, the other members' personal Entries (their shares) and UX-8's similar entries.
- D-482 (the PM). The switch and the texts. On the budget's Settings tab, for every ACTIVE member with an account, whatever their role, below the owners' form (or below the facts a member sees): a section "Your payees", a checkbox "Show the other members where I paid", and under it "The payee of each family expense and income you paid or received, past ones included. Your accounts, notes and paying amounts stay private." Changing it opens `confirm`, as Archive and Delete do: on, "Show your payees to the other members of “<budget>”? They will see where you paid for every family expense and income you paid or received in this budget, past ones included."; off, "Hide your payees from the other members of “<budget>”? They will no longer see them, past records included." Cancel leaves the checkbox and the server as they were, with no request. OK saves at once and says "Saved." as the owners' form does; a failure says so and puts the checkbox back. Under it, "Who shows payees": each ACTIVE member with an account, "Shown" or "Hidden", the reader marked "(you)". The texts that promise the payee stays private follow the reader's switch for that budget: the payee hints of the payment form (`FamilyCounterparties.tsx`), of New entry's family path and of the payment's own page, and the record page's. The invite page's "What they never see" adds "Where you paid (the payee), unless you choose to show it in the budget's settings"; the privacy policy says the same in both of its lists, with its date updated. D-81 gets a line: amended by D-479 to D-482. No decision number in any screen's text (D-381).
- D-483 (the PM). Storage, the API, the rollback. V17 adds one column to `ledger_member`: a boolean, NOT NULL, default false, which a check allows to be true only for a SHARED ledger's ACTIVE member with an account; leaving, being removed and "Delete all my data" set it false. One endpoint for the reader's own membership, any role (`PATCH /api/family-ledgers/{id}/me` with `{ "showsPayees": true }`); no request can name someone else's membership. The family budgets' answers give the reader's own `showsPayees`; the members' list gives each ACTIVE member's. The records' answers, the list and one record, give `paymentPayee`, the name, to whoever D-481 allows, and leave it out for everyone else; `yourPayment.payeeId` stays. The read of the payer's entry and payee goes through the family access layer, as the other cross-ledger reads do (F2b, the ArchUnit rules); no new path into a personal ledger. Rollback to `2e34237` stays possible: V17 stays, UX-8's code doesn't read the column, and every payee is hidden again, the safe side.
- D-484 (the agent, UX-9). The endpoint is `PATCH /api/family-ledgers/{ledgerId}/me` (`FamilyLedgerController.updateOwnMembership`), body `{"showsPayees": true|false}`, the field required (400 without it), answering the budget as the caller sees it (`FamilyLedgerView`, with `showsPayees`). It takes the scope from `LedgerAccess.member`, so a LEFT or FORMER member, someone outside the budget and any personal ledger get the missing ledger's 404, and nobody but an ACTIVE member with an account reaches it; the path names no member, and `PATCH /members/{id}`'s body has no such field (400). With the switch off it is unknown like the other family endpoints (34 since UX-9). It locks the ledger's row, as every membership change does.
- D-485 (the agent, UX-9). V17's check is `NOT shows_payees OR (ledger_type = 'SHARED' AND status = 'ACTIVE' AND user_sub IS NOT NULL)`, and a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`ledger_member_shows_payees`) sets the column false when a row stops being an ACTIVE member with an account (OLD ACTIVE with a sub, NEW not), whichever code writes it: `FamilyMembershipService.leave` and `remove`, `release_family_memberships` for "Delete all my data" and the runbook's "Delete a user", and UX-8's code after a rollback, none of which needed a change. It acts only on that change, so a statement that sets it on for a seat, a LEFT or FORMER member or a personal ledger's member is refused by the check, not quietly undone.
- D-486 (the agent, UX-9). `paymentPayee` is read by `FamilyPostingService.shownPayees(LedgerScope family, recordIds)`, beside `ownPayments`, the posting service's other read of members' personal entries: an EXPENSE or INCOME record of the scope's ledger, the PAYMENT link of its payer that isn't detached, its entry in that payer's own personal ledger (the member's sub, as `ownPayments` finds the caller's), and the entry's payee in that ledger, while the payer is ACTIVE with a sub and is the reader or shows their payees. `FamilyRecordService.views` asks it for every answer it builds (the list, one record, and the answers of a create, a change or a settlement), so a payer's own payee comes back on their create's answer too. A deleted record is never listed. No new repository or endpoint reads a personal ledger, and `ArchitectureTests` pass unchanged.
- D-487 (the agent, UX-9). `recordCategory` is given for every non-detached link whose record has a category, a share's too (where it equals the share's own categorized line), so the frontend reads one field; the Entries cell uses it only where `describeEntry` finds no categorized line. The Category filter's new branch is for entries without a categorized line only, so that a row is found by exactly the category its cell shows: a share keeps matching by its posting, a payment by its record.
- D-488 (the agent, UX-9). The Activity line keeps its wording and adds the payee after it: "Paid by you · Odido", "Paid by Sergey Zolotko · Odido", "Received by Ben · Bakery" for an income's receipt and a refund, rather than D-481's example "Sergey Zolotko paid · Odido", which would change the line every record shows (and the specs that read it) for the sake of the few with a payee. The PM may ask for the example's word order.
- D-489 (the agent, UX-9). On the record's page the payer's payee moves from under "Paid from" to the record's own "Payee" line, once, from `paymentPayee`, with "Only you see it." or "The other members see it."; the old line under the account stays only for an answer without `paymentPayee` (an api before UX-9 during a deploy). The hints: off, as before; on, the payment forms' "Where it was paid, such as a shop. It stays on your own entry; the other members of this budget see its name, as you chose in its settings." and New entry's label "Payee (the other members see it)". New entry and the payment's own page read the switch from the family budgets' list, `GET /api/family-ledgers`, which the app reloads after a save.
- D-490 (the agent, UX-9). The Settings section: its checkbox is disabled while a save runs; "Who shows payees" lists the ACTIVE members with an account from the members' answer, in its order (join date); after a save the budget, its members and the family budgets' list load again (`useFamilyMutation`), so every hint and "Who shows payees" follow at once. A failure shows the server's message.
- D-491 (the agent, UX-9). The privacy policy's lists: "What other members of a family budget see" ends with "where a member paid (the payee) for the family expenses and incomes they paid or received, only if that member chooses to show it in the budget's settings."; "What other members never see" adds "where you paid (the payee), unless you choose to show it in the budget's settings;". The sentence before that list, "Other members never see anything of your personal budget:", stays, the item saying the exception. Last updated 11 November 2026. `docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md` follows word for word (`privacy.test.ts`).
- D-492 (the agent, UX-9). End to end: `09-payees` (the specs' pattern is `0[2-9]` now), with `E2E_FAMILY=on` only: A's expense is saved from New entry with its Family expense switch and a payee typed for the first time ("Odido UX9"); the confirmation is answered through Playwright's `dialog` event (accepted, dismissed, accepted); the Entries row is found by its amount and kind; the family category's link on the Categories page lists the payment and the share.

Figures and screenshots in `data/private/reports/UX-9/`, not in the repository.
- D-494 (the PM, earlier deploys). The PM hasn't seen the output of UX-8's and UX-9's deploys. UX-10 records nothing of them and leaves "Production now", "Deployed revisions" and CLAUDE.md's Deployment line as they are; a later stage records UX-8, UX-9 and UX-10 together. UX-10's pre-flight prints the running images in full, as UX-9's did (D-452).
- D-495 (the owner). The app takes the look of design C: IBM Plex Sans for text, IBM Plex Mono for amounts, panels with a thin border, a lavender accent; three themes, Blue, Light and Dark, chosen in Settings; a permanent navigation of the pages in place of the "My finances" menu, while the user's name stays a button that opens Settings and Log out; "Finance Tracker" stays a text wordmark.
- D-496 (the PM). The themes' tokens: CSS custom properties in `index.css` on `:root[data-theme="blue"]`, `[data-theme="light"]` and `[data-theme="dark"]`, with `color-scheme: dark` (Blue, Dark) or `light` (Light), so that native controls follow. Every colour of the stylesheet and of the components comes from these tokens; no hex or rgb outside the three blocks. Existing names keep their meaning where they match; `--error-bg` becomes `--danger-bg`. The values, Blue / Light / Dark: `--bg` #18212F / #F4F6F9 / #17181B (the page); `--surface` #1F2A3B / #FFFFFF / #1F2024 (the header, panels, tables, the user's menu); `--surface-raised` #26344A / #EEF1F6 / #292A2F (fields, a pressed toggle, a hovered row, total cells); `--border` #33435C / #D5DCE6 / #393B41 (dividers, panel borders); `--border-control` #6B82A8 / #7A8599 / #787B84 (the borders of fields, buttons and the menu); `--text` #E3E9F2 / #1B2433 / #E8E8EB; `--muted` #9AA8BD / #566173 / #A4A6AD (labels, dates, secondary lines); `--accent` #A99BFF / #5B48D6 / #B3A8FF (links, the current tab, focus, the primary button); `--on-accent` #16122E / #FFFFFF / #17142E; `--income` #6BB8F2 / #0B6E99 / #74B9EE; `--expense` #F2A65A / #A55A0F / #F0A860; `--danger` #F49A9A / #B42318 / #F49A9A; `--danger-bg` #3A2630 / #FDECEA / #3B2427; `--notice-bg` #3A3424 / #FFF3D1 / #37311F (with `--text`); `--success` #7FD6B8 / #1C6A4D / #82D3B5; `--success-bg` #1D3A36 / #E4F3EC / #1E3530; `--stale` #E8C36A / #875200 / #E3BE66; `--chart-income` #4A9BE0 / #1D64A6 / #4A9BE0; `--chart-expense` #F2A65A / #D9782A / #F0A860; `--chart-income-before` #8CC3F0 / #1D64A6 / #8CC3F0 (the earlier period's dashed line); `--chart-expense-before` #F7C795 / #D9782A / #F5C690; `--chart-grid` #2E3C52 / #E3E8EF / #2C2D32.
- D-497 (the PM). The choice belongs to the browser: `localStorage` key `finance-tracker.theme`, value `blue`, `light` or `dark`, every read and write in try/catch, any other value no choice; not on the server (a theme is per device; on the account, as the date format is, would be a later stage). With no choice the app follows the device, `prefers-color-scheme: dark` Blue and otherwise Light, live while nothing is chosen. `frontend/public/theme.js` sets `data-theme` on `<html>` before the first paint, loaded in `<head>` as a classic blocking script `<script src="/theme.js"></script>`: no inline script, since Caddy's CSP is `script-src 'self'` and stays; nginx serves it under `location /` with `no-cache`, as `index.html`. `theme.ts` owns every change after that: `useTheme()` gives the theme, whether it was chosen, and `setTheme`. The landing page and `main.tsx`'s notices take the same theme; `public/privacy.html` with `privacy.css` stays as it is.
- D-498 (the owner; the details the PM's). The header supersedes D-433's and D-434's main menu and D-436's "← My finances"; the user's menu of D-433, D-434 and D-464 stays, and so does D-435's rule of no "New entry" inside a family budget. `Header.tsx` is two rows, both always shown. Row 1: on the left the wordmark "Finance Tracker", a link to `/`; on the right "New entry" (primary, `/entries/new`, not inside a family budget), then the user's menu with UX-7's behaviour unchanged (the name cut with an ellipsis, its `title` the whole name, a chevron; under 40rem the person icon and the chevron, the name as the panel's first line; Settings and Log out; one `Disclosure` with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, closed by Escape with the focus back, a click outside, an item and a route change; items rendered only while open), restyled: the button a default button; the panel `--surface` with a 1px `--border-control` border, radius 8px, no shadow; items 40px high, `--surface-raised` on hover; the current page `aria-current` in `--accent`. Row 2, `<nav aria-label="Main">`: Dashboard (`/`, `end`), Entries, Accounts, Categories, Rates; with the family switch on a thin divider, each family budget by name, sorted as now, with the shared icon and current on any of its pages, then "New family budget" (`/family/new`) with a plus icon. The current link has `aria-current="page"`, `--text`, weight 600 and a 2px `--accent` line under it (an inset box-shadow); the others are `--muted`; targets 44px high. Inside a family budget its tab is current and its `nav.subnav` stays under the page heading; no "← My finances" (Dashboard is a tab) and no "New entry". Under 40rem row 1 is one row (the wordmark, "New entry" as text, the user's button) and row 2 scrolls sideways inside itself (`overflow-x: auto`) with the current link scrolled into view; the page never scrolls sideways. `MainPanel`, `MenuIcon` and the main menu's styles go. `App.tsx`'s family list logic stays: a budget just joined shows, one just left goes, before the list reloads.
- D-499 (the owner; the details the PM's). The theme is chosen only in Settings, with no control in the header or anywhere else. Settings gets a part "Appearance" (`/settings/appearance`); `SETTINGS_PARTS` becomes Time zone, Date format, Appearance, Import, Export, Delete all my data. Its `h3` is "Theme", above a radio group of three options, each a card with the name, a line and a small preview of that theme's `--bg`, `--chart-income`, `--chart-expense` and `--accent`: Blue "Dark navy, the look the app was designed in"; Light "White panels on a pale grey page"; Dark "Neutral graphite, no blue tint". Under the group: "Saved in this browser. Until you choose, the app follows your device: Light when it is set to light, Blue when it is set to dark." Choosing applies at once, with no Save button and no message.
- D-500 (the PM). Type: `@fontsource/ibm-plex-sans` (400, 500, 600) and `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono` (400, 500), imported in `main.tsx`, the latin, latin-ext and cyrillic subsets only; no Google Fonts and no other CDN; fallbacks `system-ui, sans-serif` and `ui-monospace, monospace`. Body text 14px/20px from 40rem; under 40rem 15px, with inputs, selects and textareas 16px so that iOS doesn't zoom. Pages keep their heading levels: a page's `h2` 20/28, 600; a card's `h3` 14/20, 600; the dashboard's headline figures Mono 24/32, 500; small figures Mono 16; field and group labels 12/16 `--muted`. Plex Mono, with tabular figures, sets `.amount` everywhere, the headline figures, the period totals and the total rows, the chart's tick labels and the tooltip's amounts, the Entries date cell and DateInput's text field; everything else is Sans. Only the font changes: the money format, the signs and the date formats (D-304) stay, and there is no currency column.
- D-501 (the PM). Colour roles: income amounts `--income` and expenses `--expense`, where they are green and red today; a change against a period, better `--income`, worse `--expense`, as the mapping is now. `--danger` only for errors, refusals, `button.danger`, "rate missing" and an unbalanced entry; `.expense` and `.error` no longer share a colour. Notices `--notice-bg` with `--text`; success `--success-bg` with `--success`; errors `--danger-bg` with `--danger`; stale rates `--stale`. `IncomeExpenseChart.tsx`: bars `--chart-income` and `--chart-expense`, the earlier period's dashed lines `--chart-income-before` and `--chart-expense-before`, grid and axis `--chart-grid`, ticks `--muted`, the hover cursor `--surface-raised`, the dots' stroke `--surface`, the tooltip `--surface` with a `--border` border and `--text`; through `var(...)`, so that a theme change recolours a chart without a reload (a prop that doesn't take `var()` reads the computed value and re-renders on the change, named in the report). Amended by D-648 to D-650 (UX-16): the chart is HTML and CSS: bars `--chart-income` and `--chart-expense`, the baseline `--chart-grid`, the comparison's marker `--text` edged with `--surface`, values `--text`, "—" `--muted`, a value at or below 0's stub `--border`, a pointed row `--surface-raised`; `--chart-income-before` and `--chart-expense-before` stay defined, used by no chart.
- D-502 (the PM). Components: the page `--bg`, at most 1440px wide, with 24px sides (16px under 40rem), the header the full width; `.card` a panel (`--surface`, a 1px `--border` border, radius 8px, padding 14px 16px, no shadow); buttons radius 6px, at least 36px high (44px under 40rem): default `--surface` with a 1px `--border-control` border and `--text`, primary `--accent` with `--on-accent` and no border, danger transparent with `--danger` border and text, `button.link` `--accent`, disabled 0.55; fields (input, select, textarea, DateInput) `--surface-raised`, a 1px `--border-control` border, radius 6px, at least 36px high, `--text`, placeholders `--muted`, an invalid field a `--danger` border; `.toggles` joined segments in one 1px `--border-control` frame with radius 6px, the pressed one `--surface-raised`, `--text`, 600 with a 2px `--accent` line inside its bottom, the others `--surface` and `--muted`, `.toggle-label` visible, 12px `--muted`; the entry form's `.tabs` and `nav.subnav` as the header's row 2, 40px high; tables' header cells 12px `--muted` 500, row borders `--border`, clickable rows `--surface-raised` on hover, sticky cells `--surface`, total cells `--surface-raised`; every focusable element a 2px `--accent` outline with a 2px offset on `:focus-visible`; badges a 1px `--border` border, `--muted`, radius 4px; the footer `--muted` on `--bg`.
- D-503 (the PM). What UX-10 doesn't do: no dashboard restructuring (the same blocks in the same order, with the new look; the design's five-figure strip and its daily spending are UX-11, which needs a daily report on the server, the cash flow report being monthly); no keyboard shortcuts and no search in the header; no screen text changes but the header's "My finances" and "← My finances" going (D-498) and Settings' Appearance (D-499). The landing page uses the tokens; its two screenshots, `src/assets/demo-dashboard.webp` (1092×618) and `demo-entries.webp` (1092×462), are retaken from the demo ledger in the Blue theme at the same sizes, their alt text updated only if what they show changed. No decision number in any screen's text (D-353).
- D-504 (the PM). Guards: a unit test reads the three token blocks from `index.css` and checks WCAG 2.x contrast (`--text`, `--muted`, `--accent`, `--income`, `--expense`, `--danger` and `--stale` on `--bg`, `--surface` and `--surface-raised` at least 4.5; `--on-accent` on `--accent`, `--danger` on `--danger-bg`, `--success` on `--success-bg`, `--text` on `--notice-bg` at least 4.5; `--border-control` on the three grounds, and the four chart colours on `--surface`, at least 3), a token missing from a block failing it; a second test, like `screenTexts.test.ts`, fails on any hex or `rgb(` colour in `src/**/*.ts(x)` outside the tests, and in `index.css` outside the three blocks.
- D-505 (the agent, UX-10). The fonts are one file of the app's, `src/fonts.css`, written from the packages' own `400.css`, `500.css` and `600.css`: for each weight the `@font-face` of the latin, latin-ext and cyrillic subsets with its `unicode-range`, `woff2` only. The packages' per-subset files (`latin-400.css` and the like) have no `unicode-range`, so three of them for one weight would let the last declared serve every character; with the ranges a browser downloads a subset only for text that uses it. 15 files, 8.4 to 24.3 kB each.
- D-506 (the agent, UX-10). The Light block's selector also takes `:root:not([data-theme])`, so a page whose `theme.js` didn't run reads as Light rather than without colours, and each block also takes `.theme-preview[data-theme="…"]`, so that Appearance's three previews show their own theme's tokens whatever the page's. The guards find the blocks by `:root[data-theme="…"]` in the selector.
- D-507 (the agent, UX-10; D-501's question). Every colour prop of the chart takes `var()`: Recharts writes `fill` and `stroke` as SVG presentation attributes (the bars, lines, dots, grid, axis, the legend's icons) and the cursor as an attribute, the tooltip is the app's own HTML, and the ticks' font is a `fontFamily` attribute; all resolve against the page's custom properties, so a theme change recolours the chart without rendering it again. None reads a computed value; only the canvas that measures the ranges' axis labels (D-414) reads `--font-mono`'s value, since a canvas font doesn't take `var()`.
- D-508 (the agent, UX-10). `theme.ts`: `startTheme()`, which `main.tsx` calls before anything renders, applies the theme and listens to the device's setting and to another tab's choice (the `storage` event); a choice the storage refuses applies to this page only. `useTheme` is a `useSyncExternalStore` over it. `public/theme.js` repeats the rules in plain ES5 (no module, no build), and `theme.test.ts` runs both through the same nine cases.
- D-509 (the agent, UX-10). The header's details: the wordmark is as high as the buttons beside it (36px, 44px under 40rem), so that row 1's three items share one top; row 2 brings its current link into view by setting its own `scrollLeft` (`scrollIntoView` would scroll the page too); a budget's shared icon in row 2 is decorative (`aria-hidden`), so the link's name stays the budget's; "New family budget" is written without D-433's ellipsis, as D-498 names it. `SharedIcon` moved to `components.tsx` (`SharedMark` uses it), so the header doesn't pull the cash flow table into the main chunk.
- D-510 (the agent, UX-10). Plex Mono (D-500) sets `.amount`, `.figure dd` (the headline and small figures, the period totals), `td.total`, the subtotal and net rows' cells, `tfoot td`, the tooltip's amounts, the Entries date cell, DateInput's text field and the chart's ticks; a column heading with `.amount` (`thead th.amount`) stays Sans, being a label. Table header cells' 12px `--muted` 500 is `thead th`, not a row's `th[scope="row"]`.
- D-511 (the agent, UX-10). Buttons are `inline-flex` (an icon and text centred), `button.link` excluded from the 36px and 44px minimum; the toggles' segments are 34px (42px under 40rem) inside the frame's border, the frame `overflow: hidden` and the focus outline inset (`-2px`) so that it isn't clipped; the user's panel items are aligned to the start.
- D-512 (the agent, UX-10). End to end: the specs' pattern is `(0[2-9]|1\d)-…` so that `10-themes` runs, last; it runs in `local-specs` and `local-narrow` and checks the 375 px pages in both (its screenshots only in `local-narrow`, by `shot()`), and in production. `01-pages` checks `/theme.js` with `no-cache` on both targets, its type `application/javascript` from nginx (checked on the web image built locally) and `text/javascript` from Vite. `fixtures.ts`'s `mainMenu` returns row 2's `nav` and `openSettings` takes Appearance, whose heading is Theme.
- D-513 (the agent, UX-10). Section 3's screenshots come from `e2e/scripts/screens.ts` (`npm run screens:local -- <folder> <label> [theme]`): local only (`requireLocalOnly`), it deletes testuser's data, loads the demo, takes each screen at 1280 and 375 px with the theme set in the browser before the first page, and deletes his data again; `e2e:prod` never runs it. The "before" set is of `1417b8f` in the browser's light scheme (there was no theme then), with Settings' Time zone in place of Appearance.
- D-514 (the agent, UX-10; finding). The dev stack's frontend container keeps its own `node_modules` (an anonymous volume), so the new font packages reached Vite only after `./dev.sh -V`, which renews the anonymous volumes; the named `local-db` volume is untouched.
- D-515 (the agent, UX-10). The landing page's screenshots are taken at 1116 px in the Blue theme, the browser in Europe/Amsterdam (testuser's zone, so no zone hint shows), clipped to 1092×618 from the dashboard's headline row ("This month", the cash flow table below the chart hidden for the shot so that no heading is cut at its edge) and to 1092×462 from the Entries table's header, as the earlier ones were framed, and saved as WebP (quality 82); both alt texts name what they now show (the figures, the theme, the month and its comparisons; the family settlement, payment and share among the entries).
- D-516 (the agent, UX-10). `deploy/checks/UX-10.sql` and `UX-10.expected` are UX-9's structure check (V17's column, check and trigger, and the highest migration 17) under UX-10's name, since UX-10 changes no schema.
- D-517 (the PM, on UX-10's review; recorded in UX-11). UX-10's `npm run test:guards` failed once while `npm run e2e:local` was running, and passed 37 of 37 run alone. It isn't UX-10's doing; if it fails run alone, it gets a stage of its own. From UX-11 on, the guards run alone, after `e2e:local`, and the report gives both.
- D-518 (the PM). Stage names: this stage is UX-11; D-503's five-figure strip and daily spending become UX-12.
- D-519 (the PM). Deploys and the checklist's template. As D-494, UX-11 records nothing of UX-8's, UX-9's and UX-10's deploys: the runbook's "Production now" and "Deployed revisions" and CLAUDE.md's Deployment line stay as they are, and a later stage records the three together. The pre-flight prints the running images in full. Template (CLAUDE.md, "Deploy checklists"): a checklist's expected Flyway line in the api's log is the one that container's own start printed, `Successfully applied … migration` only when that start applied it, otherwise `Schema "app" is up to date. No migration necessary.` (UX-10's checklist expected the latter from UX-9's container, which had applied V17). For UX-11 the pre-flight reads UX-10's container, up to date since V17 was there, and the new api container after the deploy is up to date too.
- D-520 (the PM). `npm audit` and `npm audit --omit=dev` in `frontend/` and `e2e/`, read only, reported per advisory (the package and its severity, the path that pulls it in, whether it ships in the web image's bundle or is dev only, the fix: `npm audit fix` without `--force`, or a major). No dependency change in UX-11; the PM decides on the report (D-543).
- D-521 (the owner; the details the PM's). The dashboard's default period is the month of the latest entry: a new preset `latest-entry-month`, "Month of the latest entry", the calendar month of the ledger's latest entry dated on or before the user's today (`useToday`), this month with no such entry. It is first in `PRESETS` and the default: a link to `/`, and a missing or unknown `period` (`OLD_PRESETS` aside), mean it. `this-month` stays a preset as it is; a bookmark `period=latest-entry-month` stays current as entries come. The latest entry comes from `GET /api/entries?size=1&to=<today>`, newest first; no new endpoint. The page sends no report request for another period before it knows the month. Everything else follows the range as for any whole month: the comparisons (D-395), a month that runs to date when it is this month (D-396), balances as of To (D-430, D-443). No setting for it; loading the demo still opens the demo's whole period. Amended by D-692 (UX-17, the owner to confirm): after "Load demo data" the dashboard opens "Past year" (`/?period=past-year`), no longer the demo's whole period.
- D-522 (the owner; the details the PM's). Entries' periods are the dashboard's: "All time" (Entries' default, no dates), the dashboard's presets in its order and with its labels (`PRESETS`, `PERIOD_LABELS` and `presetRange` from one place that both pages use), then "Custom" (disabled, shown chosen for typed dates). Entries' own "Last month" and "Last year" go. The URL keeps a preset by name (`period=<preset>`), `from` and `to` stay for Custom, and the list's requests send the preset's `from` and `to`. Typing From or To turns the period into Custom and keeps the other end. "Month of the latest entry" here is the whole ledger's, whatever the other filters. Links into Entries from Accounts, Categories and the dashboard stay as they are and open All time.
- D-523 (the owner; the details the PM's). An entry's family record's payee. An entry a family budget posted, such as a member's share, has no payee of its own, so its Payee cell showed "—" and the payee filter, the text search and the summary missed it. The entry view's `family` gets `recordPayee` (`{ name }`; additive, left out otherwise, display only, nothing stored, rule 13): the name of the payee on the payer's own payment entry of the entry's record, exactly when the reader may see that payee (D-479 to D-482, D-486): the reader is an ACTIVE member of that budget, and is the payer, or the payer is an ACTIVE member with an account who shows their payees there, the rule `paymentPayee` and `FamilyPostingService.shownPayees` follow. For EXPENSE and INCOME records, refunds included; never for a settlement, an opening balance or a correction. One SQL rule serves the entry view, the search and the summary. The Payee cell shows the entry's own payee, else its lines' counterparties, else `recordPayee`, styled as any payee; the entry's page (`ReadOnlyEntry`) shows it too. `q` matches it as it matches a payee's name; the Payee filter (`counterpartyId`) matches an entry with no payee and no counterparty on its lines when its `recordPayee` has the chosen counterparty's name, ignoring case. A hidden payee never shows, matches or counts anywhere, for any reader. Who sees a payee doesn't change, only where; the privacy policy and `payees.ts` say what members see, not where (D-541).
- D-524 (the owner; the details the PM's). Entries' summary row, right under the column headers, covering every entry the filters match on every page: Date, the number of entries and the first and last date ("214 entries · 1–31 Oct 2026", `formatRange`); Payee, the distinct payees as the cells show them (names compared ignoring case) and "n without"; Category, the distinct categories as the cells show them (the categorized lines', else the record's, D-477) and "n without"; Accounts, the distinct accounts the column names (the lines without a category, FX_EXCHANGE left out where `describeEntry` leaves it out); Amount, per currency as the rows show amounts: "In +X" (rows shown with +), "Out −Y" (rows shown with −), "Net" only with both, "Transfers Z" (rows without a sign), each only when not 0, never added across currencies. `GET /api/entries/summary` takes the list's filters, its WHERE built once for both endpoints, read only in one snapshot; the page computes the net with `money.ts`. It is asked when the filters change, not the page; while it reloads the old figures stay, dimmed; under 40rem the row is a block of labelled lines. The row's sign and amount rule now lives twice, in `describeEntry` and in the summary's SQL: the backend tests cover each kind, and the end-to-end spec compares the row with the cells.
- D-525 (the owner; the details the PM's). Accounts' columns line up: Assets, Liabilities and Equity show their columns at the same widths and left edges at every width from 40rem, with and without a comparison: one table, a `tbody` per type with a row for its name and hint and a row for its total, the type names headings that label their groups. Under 40rem each account stays a wrapping block (D-382, D-386), and nothing scrolls sideways at 375 px.
- D-526 (the owner; the details the PM's). Accounts on a date, and compared: "Balances on" a `DateInput`, the user's today by default (`on=`, left out for today), and "Compare", which adds "Compared with" (`compare=`, by default the last day of the month before the first date's month) and a button that ends the comparison. Data: `/api/reports/balances?asOf=<day>` for each day and "By person" from `/api/reports/counterparty-balances?…&asOf=<day>`; no new endpoint. In a comparison the columns come in date order, whichever date was typed first: the earlier day's balance, the later day's, "Change" (later − earlier, the PM's addition) and "%" against the earlier day (`dashboard.changeOf` as it is, not the net's rule: "—" across 0 and from 0), per currency, for every account, each type's total and "By person"; the headers name the days in the user's date format; no colour for better or worse. An account is listed while it is listed on either day (`listedInBalances`), or with "Show archived". Rename, Archive and Restore stay; archiving still looks at every posting (D-366).
- D-527 (the owner; the details the PM's). Icon buttons on the Categories page: Rename, Archive and Restore become buttons with a pencil, a box with an arrow down and a box with an arrow up, and "Change it in the family budget" a link with an arrow leaving a box; each square, 36px (44px under 40rem), its accessible name and `title` the action and the category ("Rename Groceries", "Change Groceries in the family budget Home"). Inline SVG in a new `icons.tsx`, `currentColor` and `aria-hidden`, as `SharedIcon`; no new dependency. The rename form's Save and Cancel keep their words, Archive's confirmation stays; Accounts and a family budget's categories keep their words.
- D-528 (the PM). What UX-11 doesn't do: no migration (Flyway stays at V17); no change to a report's figures, the dashboard's blocks (the strip is UX-12), import and export, New entry's similar entries or the family budget's pages; no decision number in any screen's text (D-353) or the API's messages (D-384).
- D-529 (the agent, UX-11). `RecordPayees` (`ledger/`) is D-523's one rule: a `LEFT JOIN LATERAL` on the entry `e` that gives `rp.name`, with the reader's sub and the switch as parameters. `EntryService.families` joins it for `family.recordPayee`, and `EntryService.matching` builds the FROM and WHERE of both `search` and `summary` with it. It applies to every non-detached SHARE or PAYMENT link of an EXPENSE or INCOME record, so the payer's own payment entry gets it too, equal to the entry's own payee; the cell shows the entry's own first, so nothing changes there. A settlement's sides (link SETTLEMENT), an opening balance and a correction (no record) never match.
- D-530 (the agent, UX-11). With the family switch off, `recordPayee` is never given and never matched (the parameter `rpOn` is false), as `paymentPayee` isn't served then; the entry's `family` itself is unchanged (`FamilySwitchOffApiTests`).
- D-531 (the agent, UX-11). `q` matches `rp.name` like the entry's payee's name, whatever else the entry has; the Payee filter matches it only for an entry with no payee and no counterparty on its lines, as D-523 says, so that both find exactly what the cells show. Names are compared with PostgreSQL's `lower()`.
- D-532 (the agent, UX-11). The summary's answer (`EntrySummary`): `count`, `firstDate`, `lastDate` (null for none), `payees`, `withoutPayee`, `categories`, `withoutCategory`, `accounts`, and `currencies`, a list sorted by currency of `{currency, incoming, outgoing, transfers}` as decimal strings at or above 0 (`in` is a Java keyword). Payees are counted per name, so a cell listing two counterparties counts both; categories and accounts by id, so a personal and a family category of one name count twice; FX_EXCHANGE is the ledger's account of that code. The sign follows `describeEntry` exactly: the net of an entry's categorized lines summed across its currencies, below 0 In, above 0 Out, else Transfers, and per currency the sum of its positive lines.
- D-533 (the agent, UX-11). UX-9's tests that said a payee never reaches the other members' own entries (`FamilyPayeeApiTests.bobNeverSeesItElsewhere`, `DataIsolationApiTests.aPayeeIsShownOnlyByItsPayersChoice`) now allow it there only as the share's `family.recordPayee` while the payer shows payees, and still find it nowhere else; hidden, they find it nowhere, as before.
- D-534 (the agent, UX-11). `periods.ts` holds `PRESETS`, `PERIOD_LABELS`, `presetRange`, `presetNamed`, `validDate` and `useLatestEntryDay`; `dashboard.ts` re-exports the first ones. `presetRange` takes the latest entry's day for `latest-entry-month`, and `periodFromQuery` and `needsLatestEntry` read the URL. The dashboard shows "Loading the dashboard" until that day is known, and only for that preset; the answer is kept while another preset is chosen, so coming back doesn't wait again. An unknown `period` is the month of the latest entry on the dashboard (D-521) and All time on Entries, each page's default.
- D-535 (the agent, UX-11). On Entries a valid `period` wins over `from` and `to`; an old Entries link whose `from` and `to` equal a preset's days now reads as Custom, since presets are kept by name only (Entries never wrote `period` before).

The summary row is a `tbody.entry-summary` between the header and the entries, named "Summary of all matching entries", each cell with a label shown only under 40rem; its In amounts in `--income`, Out in `--expense` as the rows' tones; Net signed unless 0; the dates through `formatDayRange` in the browser's locale, as the dashboard's range names (`rangeLabel`), not the user's date format. `describeEntry`'s payee takes `recordPayee` as its last fallback, so the cell, the entry's page and every other user of it read the same.

- D-548 (the PM). The backend. `GET /api/entries/family-payees`, read only: the distinct names of `family.recordPayee` over the user's personal ledger's entries, by `RecordPayees`' rule, names compared with `lower()` (D-531), each with the family budgets it comes from (`id`, `name`); sorted by name ignoring case; `[]` with the switch off or without family links; no paging; nothing stored. `payee` on `GET /api/entries` and `/summary`: a name, trimmed, blank as absent, at most 100 characters (else 400); the entries whose Payee cell shows it: the entry's payee's name, or a line's counterparty's name, or for an entry with neither its shown `recordPayee`, all by `lower()`. The payee on the family forms is typed freely, with suggestions: `CounterpartyFields`' payee, so the new expense or income (`FamilyExpenseForm.tsx`), the record page's form (`EditRecord`) and the payment entry (`PaymentEntry.tsx`); the account's required counterparty (D-80) stays a list, and a settlement still has no payee. The field is `CounterpartyInput` (a text input with a datalist), opened with the current payee's name; the suggestions the user's counterparties not archived and D-548's names, one per name ignoring case, sorted; the hint as before (`payeeHint`), plus "New payee: it is added when you save." for a name no counterparty of the user's has. On save, by the trimmed name: empty removes the payee (`payeeId: null`) if there was one; the payee it had (ignoring case): nothing sent; a counterparty of the user's (archived included): its id; any other name: the user's counterparty is created first (`POST /api/counterparties`) and its id sent, remembered for a second save before the list reloads (D-462); a failure after the creation leaves the counterparty and shows the error as before. A name from the partner's payees becomes the user's own counterparty, in his ledger only, and stays his if the partner later hides his payees. Typing changes which payee the record has; it never renames an existing counterparty.

- D-570 (the PM). The daily report, read only, in `ReportService`, one statement per answer, nothing stored (rule 13): `GET /api/reports/daily-cash-flow?from=&to=` with the cash flow's `scope` and `familyLedgerId` and their rules (D-302, D-308, D-421; a family scope with the switch off 400): rows `{day, categoryCode, categoryName, categoryType, currency, total, familyLedgerId?, familyLedgerName?}`, the cash flow's rows with the day in place of the month, by the same classification and the same family categories, so that over any range and scope the days' totals per category and currency add up exactly to `/cash-flow`'s. `currency=BASE` (a second handler on the path): `{currency, rows}`, each row the day's converted `total` (each posting at its own day's rate, rounded once per category and day, null with `missingRates` when a rate is missing) and its `rates`; no exchange results; over a range the converted days may differ from the monthly figures by their rounding only. `from` after `to`, or a range of more than 366 days: 400. Tests: the sums against `/cash-flow` in each currency and every scope, BASE with a missing rate, the 400s, the switch off, another user's ledger never.
- D-571 (the owner, D-503; the details the PM's). Daily spending, as design C. Inside the card "Income and expenses", beside the chart from 64rem (the chart about two thirds), under it below; in each-currency mode one panel per currency, beside that currency's chart. The days: the section's days (D-396) when the period has at most 31 days; otherwise the calendar month of the section's last day, from the period's From if that is later; requested from the first shown day to the earlier of the last and today, with the page's scope and currency mode; days after today, up to the shown range's end, empty dashed slots (`--border`), not requested. Title "Daily spending, November 2026" for a whole calendar month, else "Daily spending, <the range>" by `rangeLabel`/`formatRange`. A day's spending is its EXPENSE rows' total (a refund lowers it, D-79): a bar per day in `--chart-expense`, its height by the shown range's largest day, 72px high (60px under 40rem); a day at or below 0 a 2px `--border` line; a day the base currency can't convert no bar, titled "rate missing", its missing rates in the page's notice. Plain HTML and CSS, no chart library. Under the bars the first day's number, today's while the range runs, and the last day's, Mono, `--muted`; then "Biggest day: <day>, <amount>, <its largest category>" (ties: the earlier day, the category first by name; a family category with its budget as the table names it), or "No spending in this period." when no day is above 0. The bars `aria-hidden`, with a text alternative a screen reader gets in full (a visually hidden table of each shown day with spending and its amount, and the biggest day); each bar's `title` its day and amount. Its own load and error: the chart and the table never wait for it. Amended by D-686 (UX-17): daily spending isn't shown for a currency none of whose shown days has a figure, nor before a day is asked; the chart then takes the row's whole width.

- D-573 (the PM). What UX-13 doesn't do: no migration (Flyway stays at V17); the rest of design C's dashboard stays out (Balances beside the chart, the categories' share bars, a Rates card, a period as buttons); no change to the Balances and Open loans cards, the cash flow table, the chart, Entries or the family budget's pages, to import and export; no decision number in a screen's text (D-353) or the API's messages (D-384).
- D-574 (the agent, UX-13). D-571's days as built (`daily.ts`, `dailyRange`): a period of at most 31 days shows the section's days, so while it runs it ends today and has no slot to come ("Daily spending, Nov 1 – 12, 2026" for this month on the 12th); a longer period shows the month of the section's last day from the period's From when that is later and, the agent's addition, to the period's To when that is earlier, so the panel never shows a day outside the period (a past custom range of 20 August to 15 October shows 1 to 15 October). So the dashed slots appear for a longer running period (a quarter or a year: this month's days after today). Nothing is asked when the first shown day is after today (a period in the future: every slot to come).
- D-575 (the agent, UX-13). D-570's range counts both ends: 366 days answer (a leap year's whole year, or 1 January to 1 January), 367 are a 400, "The daily cash flow covers at most 366 days."; a missing `from` or `to` and any other `currency` are 400 as for `/cash-flow`.
- D-576 (the agent, UX-13). One SQL builder for both reports in each currency (`ReportService.flowRows`, its bucket the month's first day or the day) and one CATEGORY part for both in the base currency (`categorySums`, which `cashFlowInBase` and `dailyCashFlowInBase` share), so the daily report classifies exactly as the cash flow by construction; `/cash-flow`'s statements and answers are unchanged.
