- D-615 (the PM). The theme's shape and how it runs. One theme, `finance-tracker`, in `auth_server/deploy/themes/finance-tracker/`, with `login/`, `account/` and `email/`, each with its `theme.properties`; parents `keycloak.v2` (login), `keycloak.v3` (account) and Keycloak's email theme; the parents' own styles and scripts stay, and ours come after them. The parent themes are read from the pinned image's themes JAR, never from memory or the web, naming the PatternFly version they use. No FreeMarker template is copied for login or account: the look comes from CSS, a small script and messages; for the email only the HTML layout template is replaced (a page that can't be made right that way: stop and ask). IDs and names stay Keycloak's (the app's e2e signs in through `#username`, `#password` and `#kc-login`). Fonts: IBM Plex Sans 400, 500 and 600, latin, latin-ext and cyrillic, `woff2`, with their `unicode-range`, the same files as the app's `@fontsource/ibm-plex-sans` 5.3.0, in the theme's resources with `OFL.txt` beside them; nothing from a CDN. Mounted read-only (`./themes/finance-tracker:/opt/keycloak/themes/finance-tracker:ro`) in `deploy/docker-compose.yml` and the dev stack's `docker-compose.yml`; files 644, directories 755, so that Keycloak (uid 1000) reads them. Production keeps the theme cache on; `sync.sh` restarts `keycloak` when a file under `themes/` changed, as it restarts caddy for the Caddyfile, and says that sign-in is unavailable for about a minute; `--dry-run` says it would; `--copy-only` doesn't restart and says a restart is needed. The dev stack's Keycloak image is production's, tag and digest.
- D-616 (the PM). Which theme a Keycloak page shows. A script in the page's head sets `data-theme` on `<html>` before the first paint: the cookie `ft_theme` when it is `blue`, `light` or `dark`, otherwise the device (dark → `blue`, light or none → `light`), following the device's changes while there is no cookie. The tokens are D-496's 22 values per theme, copied with a comment naming finance's `index.css` as their source, plus `color-scheme`; `keycloak.v2`'s own dark mode isn't used. The account console the same if `keycloak.v3` loads the script; if it can't, it follows the device by CSS alone (dark → Blue's tokens, else Light's); say which.
- D-617 (the PM). The cookie, written by the app (`frontend/src/theme.ts`): `ft_theme=<choice>; Path=/; Max-Age=31536000; SameSite=Lax`, plus `Secure` on https. `Domain` is the parent of the app's host when the host has at least three labels (`app.finance-nl.com` → `finance-nl.com`); with fewer, no `Domain`; on `localhost` a host-only cookie, which Keycloak on `localhost:8080` reads too. Written by `setTheme` on every choice, also when storage refuses it, and by `startTheme` when a stored choice exists (renewing its year and covering choices made before UX-15); never without a choice. Nothing on the server reads it; nothing else of `theme.ts` changes. The privacy policy's "Cookies" list gains it, what it holds and why, with a new date.
- D-618 (the PM). The sign-in pages: page `--bg`; a centred column at most 400px wide, 16px sides under 40rem; on top the realm's name "Finance Tracker" as Keycloak's header, Plex Sans 600, 20px, Keycloak's logo and background image gone; the form in a panel (`--surface`, a 1px `--border` border, radius 8px, padding 28px, 20px 16px under 40rem), the page's `h1` 20/28, 600. Fields: the label 13px `--muted` above; the input 40px high (44px with 16px text under 40rem), `--surface-raised`, a 1px `--border-control` border, radius 6px; the password's show button inside the field's border; an error 13px `--danger` under the field, and the field's border `--danger`. Buttons: the primary full width, 40px, `--accent` with `--on-accent`, 600; Google and GitHub side by side as default buttons (`--surface`, `--border-control`, `--text`), their icons in `currentColor`; "Or sign in with" between two `--border` lines, 12px `--muted`. Links `--accent`: "Forgot Password?" right-aligned under the password, "New user? Register" under the panel. Alerts as the app's notices (error `--danger-bg` with `--danger`, warning `--notice-bg` with `--text`, success `--success-bg` with `--success`, info `--surface-raised` with `--text`). Focus: a 2px `--accent` outline, offset 2px. The texts stay Keycloak's own, in English. Every page the realm can show: sign in, register, forgot password, update password, verify email; the info and error pages (an email verified, a link expired, the page expired) and the logout confirmation; a first sign-in with Google or GitHub (review profile, account exists: link by email or by signing in); set up and enter a one-time code.
- D-619 (the PM). The account console and the way to it. `keycloak.v3` in the tokens and Plex Sans: the masthead `--surface` with "Finance Tracker" as the wordmark in place of Keycloak's logo (by CSS, or an SVG of the text drawn as paths; say which); the navigation, forms, buttons and tables in D-618's look and the app's D-502; the current navigation item `--surface-raised` with a 2px `--accent` line on its left. The app's user menu gains "Account" between Settings and Log out: a plain link, same tab, to the console with `referrer=finance-tracker`, so that the console's "Back to Finance Tracker" returns to the client's base URL; if Keycloak wants a `referrer_uri`, it must match a valid redirect URI: add none, and say what was found. The address comes from the backend, which knows the issuer: an additive field of `/api/me` (`accountUrl`); no Keycloak address in the frontend. Under 40rem it is like the menu's other items.
- D-620 (the PM). The emails. One HTML layout for every email: a 560px panel on `#F4F6F9`, white, a 1px `#D5DCE6` border, radius 8px; a header row with "Finance Tracker", 16px 600; the body 15/23 `#1B2433`; the action as a button (`#5B48D6`, white text, radius 6px), the plain link under it in 13px `#566173`; a footer line, 12px `#566173`. Inline styles; fonts `'IBM Plex Sans', Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif`, no web font loaded; Light's colours only, in every client. The plain-text versions stay Keycloak's. The subjects "Verify your email for Finance Tracker" and "Reset your Finance Tracker password"; the others stay Keycloak's. The bodies are Keycloak's texts, with the realm's name (D-621).
- D-621 (the owner's name; the details the PM's). The realm's settings, which the checklist sets with kcadm: `displayName` = "Finance Tracker", `displayNameHtml` unset; `loginTheme`, `accountTheme` and `emailTheme` = `finance-tracker`, the client `finance-tracker` with no login theme of its own (a second app gets its own theme later); SMTP's `fromDisplayName` = "Finance Tracker" (now `finance-nl.com`), every other SMTP key unchanged: the command writes the whole `smtpServer` map as read back, changing only that key, the password staying `${vault.smtppassword}`; tried on the dev realm first and read back. The rollback values: no `displayName` (D-628 sets `myapps` instead); Keycloak 26.7's default themes, read from the image (expected `keycloak.v2`, `keycloak.v3`, `keycloak`); `fromDisplayName` = `finance-nl.com`. The dev realm gets the same settings: `realm-export.json` for a fresh volume, and a dev-only step in auth_server's README for an existing one.
- D-622 (the PM). Local checks and the deploy's order. The dev stack gets Mailpit, pinned by version and digest with its ports on 127.0.0.1 only, and the dev realm's SMTP points at it; dev only, nothing in `deploy/`. The checklist is finance's "UX-15's deploy checklist with the suite": auth first (push `auth_server`; check `/opt/auth/.deployed-revision`; `sync.sh --dry-run`, then `sync.sh`, which recreates `keycloak` for the mount, sign-in down for about a minute; the smoke test; maintenance access on; kcadm: read the current values printing no secret, set D-621's values, read them back; access off and the `401` check), then finance (`deploy.sh run <M> UX-15`, `e2e:prod`, `finish`, `verify UX-15`, the rollback acceptance, CI on Ubuntu 26.04); rollback blocks for both sides (auth: the realm's old values by kcadm and the sync backup of the files; finance: `3ed8515`, with no condition). No step between 03:30 and 04:30 UTC.
- D-623 (the agent, UX-15). What the pinned image (`quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26.7.4@sha256:82a77884…`) says, read from its JARs: `keycloak.v2` (login, `org.keycloak.keycloak-themes-26.7.4.jar`) and `keycloak.v3` (account, `keycloak-account-ui-26.7.4.jar`) use PatternFly 5 (`pf-v5-` classes, the vendor folder `patternfly-v5`; the image carries no minor version); the default themes are `keycloak.v2` for login, `keycloak.v3` for account and `keycloak` for email (`ThemeSelectorProvider`'s constants); `RealmBean.getDisplayNameHtml` falls back to `displayName`, so the header shows "Finance Tracker" with `displayNameHtml` unset; and `AccountConsole#getReferrer` turns `referrer` alone into the client's root and base URL, checking a `referrer_uri` against the redirect URIs only when one is sent. So the app sends `referrer` only and no redirect URI is added. Production's client `finance-tracker` was created with the name "Finance Tracker" and base URL `https://app.finance-nl.com/` (auth_server's PRODUCTION.md); the checklist reads both back.
- D-624 (the agent, UX-15). The account console loads the script: `keycloak.v3`'s `index.ftl` renders `scripts` from `theme.properties` as module scripts, so `js/theme.js` sets `data-theme` there too, deferred; until it runs `css/tokens.css` follows the device by CSS (`:root:not([data-theme])`, dark → Blue's tokens), which is also the sign-in pages' state without the script. The masthead's wordmark is drawn by CSS (Keycloak's logo `img` hidden, the brand link's `::after` "Finance Tracker", Plex Sans 600 20px), not an SVG.
- D-625 (the agent, UX-15). `css/tokens.css` (the fonts' faces and the three themes' tokens) is one file, generated once from finance's `frontend/src/index.css` and `fonts.css` and copied unchanged into `login/` and `account/`, and the nine font files and `OFL.txt` are in both types' `resources/fonts/`: a theme's types don't share resources without an `import` of a common theme, which would replace `keycloak.v2`'s own `import=common/keycloak`.
- D-626 (the agent, UX-15). The email layout turns the body's own link, Keycloak's `<a>` in its own paragraph, into the button by rewriting that `<a>`'s attributes in `template.ftl` (a regular expression over the body as the template renders it), and puts the plain link under it; every `<p>` gets a 16px bottom margin. The footer line: "Sent by Finance Tracker's sign-in. If you didn't expect it, you can ignore it." The layout's `meta` says `color-scheme: light only`.
- D-627 (the agent, UX-15). Keycloak 26.7 shows no password fields on the registration form while `verifyEmail` is on: the password is asked after the email is verified (`RegistrationPassword.buildPage`, unless the form's config `always_set_password_on_register_form` is set). Production has `verifyEmail` on, so its registration looks so already; the theme changes nothing of it. "New user? Register" and the other info lines (`#kc-info`) stand under the panel by CSS, the panel leaving room for them. A button without a kind of its own (`keycloak.v2` defines no default class, "Account already exists"'s two) is a default button; a link drawn as a button has its label centred.
- D-628 (the owner, UX-15, in place of D-621's "no displayName"). kcadm can't unset a realm's display name on Keycloak 26.7.4: `-d displayName` and an empty value both leave it as it is (tried on throwaway dev realms). Without one the sign-in pages show the realm's name, `myapps`, and the emails `Myapps`. The auth rollback sets `displayName` = `myapps`: the pages read as before, the emails say `myapps`.
- D-629 (the agent, UX-15). The dev realm: `realm-export.json` and the README's dev-only step also give the client `finance-tracker` the name "Finance Tracker" and the base URL `http://localhost:5173/` (the console's "Back to Finance Tracker" needs both, as production's client has them) and `testuser` the role `default-roles-myapps`, which every production user has and the account console needs (without it its API answers 401); the dev SMTP's sender is `noreply@finance-tracker.test`. Mailpit is `axllent/mailpit:v1.32.0` (2026-10-22) by its index digest.
- D-630 (the agent, UX-15). `sync.sh`: a theme change is a file under `themes/` in rsync's own list of files to send (`<f` lines); after the copy every file under `/opt/auth/themes` is set 644 and every directory 755; `keycloak` is restarted after `docker compose up -d` only when its container is the same one as before (recreated, it reads the new theme anyway: the first deploy, which adds the mount, recreates it); the note on sign-in is printed before the question, `--dry-run` says it would restart, `--copy-only` says a restart is needed with the command. As before, `sync.sh` deletes no file on the server.
- D-631 (the agent, UX-15). Keycloak's resource paths (`/resources/<version>/login/finance-tracker/…`) keep their version across a restart (checked on a throwaway 26.7.4 with the cache on), so a browser may keep an older copy of a changed file: a later change gives the changed CSS or script a new file name, named in `theme.properties` (auth_server's PRODUCTION.md, "Theme"). The first deploy is unaffected: every path is new.
- D-632 (the agent, UX-15). The cookie and the link in the app: `cookieDomain` gives no `Domain` for an IPv4 or IPv6 address either; a browser that refuses `document.cookie` leaves the sign-in pages following the device. `accountUrl` is built once from the configured issuer and client id (`app.keycloak.*`, which the backend checks every token's `iss` and `aud` against), the client id URL-encoded; the header shows Account only for an `http:` or `https:` address, and `isMe` doesn't check the field, so an older api or a bad value leaves the item out and nothing else.
- D-633 (the agent, UX-15). The privacy policy (last updated 13 November 2026): the cookie list's introduction says "strictly necessary for the service, or that keep a choice you made", the theme cookie's item says what it holds (only the chosen theme), why (the login pages show it), when it is set (only on a choice), how long (a year from the last visit), where it goes (finance-nl.com's own sites) and that no server reads it, and the consent sentence says "strictly necessary or keeps a choice you made". For the owner to review.
- D-634 (the agent, UX-15). The checks: `10-themes` reads the cookie after each choice (value, domain, `SameSite`, `Secure`) and opens Keycloak's authorization endpoint with `prompt=login` and a fixed PKCE challenge (the client requires S256), its realm from `/api/me`'s `accountUrl`, then checks `html[data-theme]` and the header and leaves the page; `13-account` (A) opens Account from the menu, checks the console's heading, theme and wordmark, and follows "Back to Finance Tracker" to `/`. `npm run screens:keycloak -- <folder>` (local only) takes every page of D-618, the console's three pages and the three emails in throwaway realms `ux15-pages` (set as production's `myapps`, with Google and GitHub shown) and `ux15-idp` (a stand-in provider, hidden, for the first sign-in pages), and deletes both.
- D-635 (the agent, UX-15). `deploy/checks/UX-15.sql` and `UX-15.expected` are UX-14's 12 lines unchanged (no migration, no table, no stored row changed). The checklist runs auth's part before `run` and, since `sync.sh` recreates `keycloak` for the mount, the sign-in pages are down for about a minute then; the finance api isn't touched by it (its users stay signed in until their next refresh, which waits for Keycloak).
- D-636 (the agent, UX-15). The checklist's kcadm reads use `--fields 'displayName,displayNameHtml,loginTheme,accountTheme,emailTheme,smtpServer(*)'`: `smtpServer` without `(*)` prints `{ }` whatever the map holds. The read prints the SMTP map's keys, its login among them and the password as the vault reference `${vault.smtppassword}`, no secret. kcadm's `update` writes the realm back from what it read with the keys given changed, so `-s 'smtpServer.fromDisplayName=Finance Tracker'` writes the whole map as read with that key changed (tried on a dev realm with a map of production's shape: only that key changed, and the rollback gave the map back exactly).

- D-647 (the owner). The chart "Income and expenses" is drawn with horizontal bars in both its forms (D-399: by range under 3 calendar months, by month from 3). Daily spending keeps its vertical columns (D-571); "Cash flow by category"'s share bars stay as they are (D-595).
- D-648 (the PM). The chart is plain HTML and CSS, as daily spending and the share bars are. `IncomeExpenseChart.tsx` keeps its name and its `ChartData` input; Recharts goes from it. The chart is no longer a lazy chunk: `Dashboard.tsx` imports it directly; its `Suspense`, its `ErrorBoundary`, "Loading chart…" and `.chart-placeholder` go, and `chunkReload.ts` and its test with them if nothing else imports them. `recharts` leaves `frontend/package.json` and the lock file if nothing else imports it; `npm audit` reported before and after. D-415's measuring and wrapping go with their tests (`axisLines`, `measureText`, `RangeTick`): the labels are HTML text and wrap by themselves. D-414's goal stays: at 375 px nothing touches or overlaps. Colours only through the tokens (D-501); D-504's guards pass unchanged.
- D-649 (the PM). The chart's anatomy, every form. A row per range, month or year (D-652), top to bottom oldest first (D-413's order). Two columns: the labels, `max-content` at most 40% of the chart's width, their text wrapping inside; then the bars. By range the role in Sans 13px `--text` (the period's own role, "Period" or "To date", 600), its range under it in Mono 12px `--muted`; by month `monthLabel`'s month in Mono 13px and `monthNote`'s note under it in 12px `--muted`. In each row two bars, Income above Expenses, from one baseline: a 1px `--chart-grid` line at the start of the bar column, the chart's full height; no value axis and no gridlines. 12px thick by range, 10px by month and by year; 2px between a row's bars; 10px between rows by range, 6px by month and by year; `--chart-income` and `--chart-expense`, square at the baseline, 4px rounded at the end. Each bar's value 6px after its end, Mono 12px `--text`, as the tables format it (`formatMoney`, `rounded` as they do); text never in a series colour. Null: no bar, "—" in `--muted`. MISSING: no bar, "rate missing" as the tables show it. 0 or below: no bar, a 2px `--border` stub at the baseline, and the value, signed. One scale per chart (per currency in each-currency mode): the largest value among its bars and its markers spans the bar column less the room of the widest value text, its characters × 0.6em + 8px, with no measuring (Plex Mono's advance), unless a glyph of a currency's format falls back to another font. The legend above the rows, 12px `--muted`: a 10px swatch with "Income", one with "Expenses", and by month with a comparison the marker with `BEHIND`'s name; "Compare with" above it. The figure keeps its `aria-label` and, in each-currency mode, its `figcaption`; the height follows the rows.
- D-650 (the PM). The comparison by month (D-399's dashed lines) becomes a marker on each bar, at the comparison month's value: a 2px line in `--text`, 4px taller than the bar (2px above, 2px below), with a 1px `--surface` edge each side. None where the comparison's month is null, MISSING, or 0 or below. When the marker lies past its bar's end, the value text follows the marker, 6px after it: a marker never crosses a value. Which comparison is drawn, the toggle and `compare=previous` don't change (`drawable`, `drawnComparison`). `--chart-income-before` and `--chart-expense-before` stay defined (Keycloak's copy, D-625; D-504's guard) though the chart no longer uses them.
- D-651 (the PM). By range every figure is on the chart: no hover panel. By month and by year, pointing at a row or tapping it gives the row `--surface-raised` and shows a panel in `.chart-tooltip`'s look beside it, inside the card: the month with its note, its income and expenses; with a comparison, the comparison's month and its income and expenses, each with the change in % (`changeOf`, `changeText`). Leaving the chart or tapping outside hides it; no `title` attributes. The drawn rows are `aria-hidden`; a visually hidden table carries every row: caption "Income and expenses in <currency>", a row per range, month or year with its income and expenses, and by month with a comparison the comparison's month, its income and its expenses. Amended by D-687 (UX-17): a comparison month with no figure in either series is left out of the panel; a series without a figure shows "—" with no "(—)".
- D-652 (the PM). A section of more than 24 calendar months is drawn by calendar year: a row per year the section touches, a year covered in part noted with its days as `monthNote` notes a month ("from 1 Dec", "to 13 Nov"). A year's income and expenses are the sums of its months' subtotals in `money.ts`'s decimals: MISSING if any month is MISSING, null if every month is null. No comparison is drawn by year and "Compare with" isn't shown; the tables keep their comparison columns. A pure helper in `dashboard.ts` (`yearRows`), unit-tested; `chartByMonth` and `monthPairs` don't change.
- D-653 (the PM). The landing page's `src/assets/demo-dashboard.webp` is retaken by D-606's recipe, since it shows the chart; its alt text in `Landing.tsx` stays true. `demo-entries.webp` stays.

- D-655 (the PM). What UX-16 doesn't change: no backend, API, migration or stored data, no import or export; every figure the chart shows is today's, from the same loads (`rangeBars`, `monthPairs`); no token's value. Rollback target `56c51bb`.
- D-656 (the agent, UX-16). D-649's glyph check, on the `@fontsource/ibm-plex-mono` 400 files the app bundles (latin, latin-ext, cyrillic): every character of the currency formats checked (`€ $ £ ¥ ₽ ₹ ₺ ₴ ₸ ₩ ₪`, digits, `, . - −`, the no-break space U+00A0, letters of "zł", "Kč", "CHF", "A$") has an advance of exactly 0.6em, except the narrow no-break space U+202F, which some locales' formats use as their group separator (French, for one) and which falls back to another font. A narrow no-break space is narrower than 0.6em in any font, so the estimate only over-counts for it and the room is never too small; the chart doesn't measure. The PM may ask for measuring in that case instead.
- D-657 (the agent, UX-16). `react-is` leaves `frontend/package.json` with `recharts`: it came in with Recharts as its peer (`2e08797`) and nothing in the app imports it; `@testing-library/dom`'s own `react-is@17` stays as its dependency. `ErrorBoundary` stays in `components.tsx`, which `Safe` uses.
- D-658 (the agent, UX-16). Placements in the bar column are CSS (`calc((100% - <room>px) * <share>)`), each share a JS number from `toChartNumber` (lengths only). A value's left is its bar's end + 6px, a marker's centre + 7px (its 1px half, then 6px) when the marker lies past the bar, 8px after a stub, 6px from the baseline for "—" and "rate missing"; the room counts every value text the chart shows, "—" and "rate missing" included. A value's box is as tall as its bar (`line-height: 1` in a box of the bar's height), so two values of a row and a marker of the other bar never overlap. A bar above 0 is at least 1px wide. The class names are `ie-*` (`ie-chart`, `ie-legend`, `ie-rows`, `ie-row`, `ie-label`, `ie-bars`, `ie-line`, `ie-bar`, `ie-stub`, `ie-marker`, `ie-value`, `ie-panel`), the form on the chart (`ie-ranges`, `ie-months`, `ie-years`). The rows are one CSS grid with subgrid rows, `fit-content(40%) minmax(0, 1fr)`, the labels `overflow-wrap: anywhere`; the rows touch and their space is the cells' padding, so the baseline runs the chart's full height and a pointed row's background covers it.
- D-659 (the agent, UX-16). The legend's third item is `BEHIND`'s name as it is ("a year earlier", "previous period", "previous year"), its first letter capitalised by CSS only, after a swatch drawn as the marker; the hover panel's comparison lines carry a 2px `--text` key, its own lines a 10px swatch of the series. The hidden table's headers: "Range", "Month" or "Year", "Income", "Expenses", and with a comparison "Month, <BEHIND>", "Income, <BEHIND>", "Expenses, <BEHIND>"; a row's header is the role and its range ("Previous period, Sep 2026"), the month or year with its note. A `<table>` ignores `width: 1px` and `overflow: hidden`, so the table sits in a `div.visually-hidden`: by month with a comparison its six nowrap columns made the page 717 px wide at 375 px until then.
- D-660 (the agent, UX-16). The hover panel (D-651) is right-aligned in the chart, under the pointed row, or over it for a row in the chart's lower half, so that it stays inside the card; it takes no pointer events. A mouse leaving the chart hides it (`pointerType` `mouse`; a touch's own pointerleave after a tap doesn't), a tap shows it, a `pointerdown` outside the chart hides it.

- D-684 (the owner, UX-17). Where nothing was posted, the dashboard shows nothing: no empty chart, no column of dashes.
- D-685 (the PM, UX-17). A comparison with nothing posted is left out, in each currency on its own (the one table in the base currency, each currency's table in each currency). "Nothing posted" in a currency: the comparison's own cash flow has no income, expense or exchange-rate line in it (no table for it, or an empty one); a line whose amounts net to 0 is something posted. Such a comparison loses, for that currency: its two columns in "Cash flow by category" (the amount and Change %); its row on the chart by range; its markers and its legend entry on the chart by month; its changes in the headline strip (D-569; already left out where they'd be "—", kept). "Compare with" offers only the comparisons `drawable` allows that at least one currency's table keeps; a `compare=previous` naming one not offered falls back as `drawnComparison` does. A currency whose table doesn't keep the drawn comparison gets no marker and no marker in its legend. The period's own column and row always stay; a category with amounts in a comparison only is still listed with "—" in the period (D-393). A comparison that failed keeps D-401: its error shown, its columns left out. No note in place of a comparison left out.
- D-686 (the PM, UX-17). Empty rows, charts and panels. By month and by year (D-399, D-652), the leading rows with nothing are left out (the period's income and expenses null, and the drawn comparison's too): the chart starts at its first row with a figure. Later rows with "—" stay; MISSING counts as a figure. A chart left without a row isn't drawn: in each currency its figure goes, and the currency's table stays. Daily spending (D-571) isn't shown for a currency none of whose shown days has a figure (nothing posted on any asked day, or no day asked yet); the chart then takes the row's full width at every width. `NoCashFlow`'s text stays for a period and comparisons with nothing in any currency.
- D-687 (the PM, UX-17; D-678's second "Later" item, which D-684 decides). In the hover panel by month, a comparison month with no figure in either series leaves the comparison's part out of the panel; where only one series has a figure, the other shows "—" with no "(—)". The item is closed in "Later"; D-678's first item stays there.
- D-688 (the owner, UX-17). The demo goes back at least a year and a month, so that it shows changes over a year.
- D-689 (the PM, UX-17). The demo has 36 months, this month included (`MONTHS` 36). Whatever day it's loaded, it then covers in full the two calendar years before this one, so every period of the Period list compares with a range the demo covers in full. Each monthly entry still falls once in every calendar month the demo covers in full; the opening balances on the 1st of its first month; nothing after today. Every entry kind stays; every entry keeps the ledger's rules; the balances stay plausible on every day and end plausibly. Invented, as before: no real people, companies or brands.
- D-690 (the PM, UX-17). The years differ, plausibly: the salary rises once a year, the rent once a year, the transit pass and a subscription now and then; electricity and heating follow the calendar (higher from November to March); the other monthly amounts vary so that no month repeats the same month a year earlier. The occasional entries recur by year where a person's would: a summer trip each year (somewhere different, partly in dollars), a birthday present, the quarterly interest, freelance projects in different months; the loan to Robin and the split purchase happen once. For every whole month that has its whole month a year earlier in the demo, the personal plan's expenses in euros differ from it by 1% to 35%, and the salary differs. The ledger is the same for the same day and may differ with the day it's loaded on.
- D-691 (the PM, UX-17). The demo family budget (H1) covers the same 36 months from the demo's first day: the monthly shops and water bills, its occasional records by year as D-690 says, and Sam paying back often enough that what Sam owes stays below a few hundred euros on every day.

- D-695 (the agent, UX-17). D-685's "nothing posted" in code: `dashboard.hasPosted`, a range's table in the currency with an income or expense line or an exchange-rate line the table shows (`exchange`, a figure other than 0 or MISSING in a month). The backend's months of 0 revaluation, where nothing was held, are no exchange-rate line: otherwise a year earlier before the demo started would keep its column in the base currency. `compareTables` builds each currency's table from the columns it keeps, so the strip's changes (`totalChanges`), the chart by range (`rangeBars`) and the table follow it; `keeps`, `offeredComparisons` (what "Compare with" offers) and `drawnComparison`'s third argument, the offered list.
- D-696 (the agent, UX-17). D-686 in code: `fromFirstFigure` trims the months of `monthPairs` and the years of `yearRows`; daily spending is shown while its rows load or fail (its own "…" and error, D-571) and left out once they answer with no figure (`daily.hasDailyFigure`: a day up to today with an expense row, a refund's below 0 included, or one without a rate; a day of income only is no figure); a currency with neither a chart nor daily spending has no row at all; the chart alone is `.chart-row.solo`, one column inside the container query.
- D-697 (the agent, UX-17). The demo's monthly entries keep their slots (a fixed 0 to 27 days before the same day of an earlier month), now 36; the account fee moved from day 1 to day 0, so that the last entry stays on the load day (`DemoLedgerView.to`). The yearly entries are on fixed calendar days within the demo's days: freelance projects on the 14th (three a year, in other months each year), pharmacy 9 Feb, the birthday present from family 14 Mar, a bike service 6 Apr, spring shoes 19 Apr, a friend's present 23 May, train tickets 24 Jun, the hotel (dollars) 3 Jul and museum tickets (dollars) 5 Jul, a dental check-up 12 Sep, a jacket 16 Oct and its partial return 27 Oct, holiday presents 15 Dec, the quarterly interest at each quarter's end. The loan to Robin (9 months back), its repayments (7 and 1 months back) and the split purchase (4 months back) are in the last year, whose months have no month a year later, so that each adds to its month's difference from a year earlier rather than taking from the next year's.
- D-698 (the agent, UX-17). D-690's levels are counted back from the load day: the salary €2,760.00 since the last April, €90 or so less for each April before (2,680, 2,590, 2,510); the rent €1,140 since the last September (1,110, 1,080, 1,050); the transit pass, the sports club and the streaming subscription by a table of the years back; every other euro amount from a table by calendar month (energy higher from November to March) at a price level about 4.5 % lower for each calendar year back, plus a step of 0 to 3 % by the year and month. So every euro expense is 1.5 % to 8 % above the same month's a year earlier, and the months' expenses differ from a year earlier by +2.8 % to +8.8 % on 2026-11-13; income moves with the freelance projects and the interest. A ledger loaded a year later has the same current salary and shows the earlier years lower. Amended by D-712 (UX-17-doc): `grown`'s step of 0 to 3 % depends on the year only (with each item's own salt), not on the month, whose term (month × 7) is 0 modulo 7; the amounts, D-690's bounds and the tests are unchanged.
- D-699 (the agent, UX-17). Amounts the demo works out from its balances, in date order: the credit card's bill pays all the card owes (so no refund can turn the card positive; the jacket and its return are on the current account now, which a debit card pays); "Monthly savings" moves what the current account holds above €3,200, rounded down to €50, between €200 and €1,500; the cash withdrawal tops cash up to about €120 (rounded up to €10, €20 to €200); the partner's share of the groceries pays all the shared account holds; the quarterly interest is 0.35 % of the savings; dollars are exchanged on 12 Feb, May, Aug and Nov when more than $1,600 are held, keeping $1,100, rounded down to $100, at €0.862 to €0.921 a dollar by year. The dollar account starts with $1,450, and FAMILY_DEBT with €84.20 the partner owed when the ledger started, so that the first month has "Partner's share of the groceries" like every other (six opening balances). With the demo family's payments and settlements the current account still never goes below zero (`DemoLedgerTests.theFamilysPaymentsLeaveTheAccountsAboveZero`).
- D-700 (the agent, UX-17). The demo family (D-691): every amount a multiple of €0.10, so an equal split has no remainder; the monthly shop (the user's, about €95 to €125, larger than before so that Sam's part is worth paying back) and water bill (Sam's); the birthday party's food on 22 Mar each year, Sam's, 60/40; a weekend away each 9 Aug in dollars from the dollar account, somewhere else each year; the bike sold once, five months back. Sam pays back every third month what Sam owes in euros, rounded down to €10, and each 20 Sep all Sam owes in dollars, into the dollar account (`DemoFamily.Settlement.currency`, passed to `NewSettlement`). `DemoFamily.owed` works out what Sam owes on a day, for the plan and its tests: between −€150 and €300 on every day of four years.

Its "Database" list has no entry for V15 (`V15__member_import.sql`: `family_import_cutoff`, `imported_family_side`) and lists the others out of order (V11, V13, V14, V16, V17, V12, V10); it says `docker-compose.local.yml` turns the family switch on, which `docker-compose.yml` does, and that `deploy/app/docker-compose.yml` doesn't set it, "so production has it off", while that file reads `${FAMILY_LEDGERS_ENABLED:-false}` from `.env` and production has it on since 2026-10-03; the project map still opens "State on 2026-09-28".
- D-738 (the PM, DOC-1b). The Supabase project in `eu-west-1` no longer exists: the owner confirmed on 2026-11-14 that it was deleted long ago (the date isn't recorded). This resolves D-729's Supabase items (whether it exists, whether its password was changed). D-720's decision, "move to Supabase: deferred, no date", stays; a move would start from a new project. `docs/database-hosting.md` and CLAUDE.md say so.
- D-739 (the PM, DOC-1c). CI #143 attempt 1 (run 43593168410, on `0442d46`, a documents-only commit that passed as CI #142 at the same time) failed in the Frontend job's Test step: `src/UX13.test.tsx`, "the headline strip (D-569) > names the scope under Income and Expenses, as Show and Records do", line 333, `expected [ '/api/settings', …(48) ] to include '/api/reports/daily-cash-flow?from=2026-10-01&to=2026-10-31&scope=FAMILY&familyLedgerId=7&currency=BASE'`. A race in the test, not in the app: the test waited for the cash flow's figures, then asserted at once that the daily spending's request had been made, which starts later. Fixed by waiting for it; every frontend assertion of a request or a late-loaded element is checked for the same race; the frontend suite run at least 5 times and `UX13.test.tsx` at least 20, with no retries added to the test configuration, which would hide races instead of fixing them.
- D-740 (the agent, DOC-1c). The race, as found: in the test's third render (`scope=family`, no `family=`) the first cash flow and daily requests go out without a budget, since the scope's one budget is known only once `/api/family-ledgers` answers; the figure €2,000.00 shows from that first answer (the stub ignores the scope), so the wait resolves early; the render that adds the scope notes also sets the daily path with `familyLedgerId=7`, and its request starts in that render's effects, which React runs after the DOM change that resolves Testing Library's next check. Shown with a throwaway change, never committed: `Dashboard.tsx` asking a later change of the daily path 50 ms after its render (the first path at once): the old line 333 failed 3 runs of 3 with CI's message, the other 22 tests passing; the fixed test passed 3 of 3. The rule applied: an assertion that a request was made is safe when the preceding wait observed that request or its result, or the request was made before the wait (in the first render, which Testing Library's `render` flushes, or in a synchronous handler); a log of `calls` right after each `render` in `UX3`, `UX11` and `UX13` showed every other asserted request there made in the first render. Changed: `UX13.test.tsx` line 333 only. Checked and left, with their reasons in DOC-1c's change log entry: `UX13` 354, 366, 377 and 384 (made in the first render), `UX3` 194 to 197, 205, 206 and 261 (the same), 278 (the alert is the PATCH's answer), `UX11` 100 and 108 (the same render as the request waited for), 264 (the first render), `Family.test.tsx` 129, 198, 250, 269 and 389 (after their own requests' results), `FamilyLifecycle.test.tsx` 88, 89, 155, 223 and 267 (the same; 267 asserts that nothing is asked), and the other files' request assertions (`Import`, `importFilter`, `FamilyPayer`, `DateInput`, `Export`, `UX9`, `StartPages`, `UX8`, `EntryForms`, `invite`, `Invite`: results waited for, or `confirm` and handlers called synchronously). The late-loaded elements read right after a wait in the five named files are rendered by the same answer or the same render as what was waited for.

## Planned stages
- F1: analysis and ADR 0003 (done).
- F2a: migration V5, stronger isolation tests, these documents; deployed on its own.
- F2b: LedgerScope and LedgerAccess, every query scoped by ledger_id, an architecture test; deployed on its own.
- F3a: migration V6 and the backend: family ledgers, members without accounts, split rule, family categories in the family ledger, the feature switch (D-25), and the membership part of D-20.
- F3b: the interface: the ledger switcher and the family pages.
- F4a: the backend of family expense records and posting: family records, shares, the posting service, expenses in the base currency, balances, read-only posted rows, the change journal, the start date (D-27); UNALLOCATED becomes a system account. Also family categories in personal ledgers: merging the creator's chosen categories into the family ones at creation, the posting trigger's exception for family categories, and family categories in personal category lists and reports. Until then a family category lives only in its family ledger.
- F4b: the interface of family expense records.
- F4c: the payer's side, backend and interface: edits of the payment fields (D-14), moving a payment to an account, the payer's own payment entry through the personal endpoints, and marking a new personal expense as family (C2). Until F4c, a wrong payment field is fixed by deleting the record and entering it again.
- F4d: settlements (D2, D-24) and family incomes (C5).
- F4e: other currencies (C7, D-13).
- F5: invites, seat claiming, returning members (D-26). The privacy policy text is ready before F5 starts.
- F6: leaving, removal and detach; delete-my-data for family data; the published privacy policy; the demo family; family reports and the personal-report filter; removing old SharedExpense creation from the UI. With the demo family, revisit the demo's personal twin of a starter category merged into a family budget (accepted after the review of F4a's parts 5 and 6).
- After the F4e review (2026-10-01): F5 is invites and taking a seat (D-17, D-18). Returning members (D-26) and making another member an owner move to F6, next to leaving and removal. The privacy policy's draft is docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md, which F6 publishes.
- After the F5 deploy (2026-10-01, decided by the PM): F6 is split in two. F6a is the membership's lifecycle (leaving, removal and detach, the rule back to equal shares, returning members, making another member an owner) and what "Delete all my data" shows of the family budgets. F6b is the privacy policy's publication, the demo family (H1), the family report (E1, and E3's check), H4, the owner's remarks on the interface, and the switcher's placeholder right after accepting an invite.
- After the F6a deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): the owner confirmed F6a as built (ADR 0003 topic J), gaps 1, 2 and 4 to 9 of the F6a report; gap 3 is replaced by D-36. F6b, in this order: D-35 to D-37; the switcher's placeholder right after accepting an invite or leaving; the members table's actions at 375 px; then the privacy policy's publication, the demo family (H1), the family report (E1, and E3's check) and H4.
- After the OPS-1 deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): F6 is split further. F6b: D-35 to D-39, the switcher's placeholder right after accepting an invite or leaving, the members table's actions at 375 px, and OPS-1's follow-ups (a read-only database role for the deploy scripts' checks, `deploy.sh adopt`). F6c, next: the privacy policy's publication, the demo family (H1), the family report (E1, and E3's check), H4 and the owner's remarks from the manual check.
- After the F6b deploy (2026-10-02): the owner confirmed F6b as built (ADR 0003 topic J, "F6b as built"), items 2 to 8 of the F6b report, and reviewed the privacy policy's draft (docs/family-budget/privacy-draft.md as of `7a60020`), which F6c publishes. F6c's plan, with the gaps it decides, is ADR 0003 topic J's "F6c plan".
- After the F6c deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): the owner confirmed F6c as built (ADR 0003 topic J, "F6c as built"): the report's shape, the demo family's content, the twins merged into the demo family rather than into the family budget they come from, H4 for creation only with the API unchanged, the legal questions kept out of the published policy, and the mapping of F6b's items (D-35 to D-39) onto what was built. F7's way of switching: a command, `deploy.sh switch on|off`, never a hand edit of `.env`; the way back is `switch off`, which leaves the data and makes the family endpoints answer 404 again. F7's check in production: done with the owner's two test accounts, never his own account, whose personal ledger is kept for his Excel import; it ends with the family budget deleted (D-36) and "Delete all my data" for both test accounts.
- F7: the switch goes on in production, followed by a check with two real accounts.
  - Prerequisite: the client address of IPv6 clients (F6a's residual risk: with an AAAA record for app.finance-nl.com and Docker's IPv6 off, every IPv6 client could reach the api as one address) is settled before the switch goes on, so that D-29's per-address limit counts the browser's real address for them too. Settled by D-38 (2026-10-02): such an address is the bridge's gateway, a private one, for which only the per-user limit applies.
- After the F7 deploy (2026-10-02, decided by the PM): F7's deploy passed every check of `deploy.sh run`, but the privacy policy answered 403 (the deploy scripts' `umask 077` wrote the changed `privacy.html` with mode 600, which the web image kept and nginx couldn't read); `finish` recorded the browser checks as passed by mistake, the switch went on, and by D-41 it was switched off again. F7b, a stage of its own: the web image readable whatever the checkout's modes, with CI building it from a copy with the server's modes; page checks in the deploy scripts (`run`, `verify`, `finish`, `rollback.sh`), and `finish` recording the browser checks as failed, without asking, when a page fails; git commands that write the working tree under `umask 022`, everything else under 077; `switch`'s text. Its checks are `deploy/checks/F7b.sql`. F7's production check moves to F7b's checklist, after its `finish` and `switch on`. F7's report claimed that the `edge` network's subnet check proves D-29's per-address limit counts real clients; it doesn't (with Docker's IPv6 off, IPv6 clients arrive as the bridge's gateway, D-38's case), and the check is dropped from the checklists.
- After F7: the Excel import, with Family rows going into a family ledger.

## Later
Found along the way; not part of a stage yet.
- D-370's cleanup (after UX-3): closed by D-381 (UX-4): both opening balances stay, and the owner clears his archived accounts' €0.01 in his own file.
- Sequential scans on posting. F2b's EXPLAIN showed the balance, integrity and rates statements reading all postings and hash-joining them with the ledger's entries, since posting has no ledger column; the same statements filtered by user_id had the same plan before F2b. Revisit after the Excel import, or when demo ledgers from open registration pile up. The options: a ledger_id on posting, checked by a trigger against its entry's; or statements that start from the ledger's entries and reach the postings through their index.
- Minimal transfers (called D3 in the F4b review). "Who owes whom" pairs the largest debtor with the largest creditor, then the next, as a display only; the model fixes each member's balance, not the transfers. The fewest transfers that settle every balance stay for later.
- The family paths (after the F4d review). The activity's paths `/family/{id}/expenses…` hold expenses, incomes and settlements; rename them to `/family/{id}/activity…`, with redirects from the old paths, which personal entries and the journal link to.
- Invites for a taken seat (after the F5 deploy). When an invite takes a seat, the other pending invites for that seat stay pending until they expire; accepting one answers 409 (D-29). Revoking them when the seat is taken stays for later.
- A long invite history. The owners' invite list keeps every invite, newest first; folding the old ones away stays for later.
- Making an owner a member again (gap 7 of the F6a report). Today an owner stops being one only by leaving or being removed; an endpoint that makes an owner a member again stays for later.
- One address for a household. The per-address rate limit of invites (D-29) counts every device behind one NAT together, such as a household's router.
- IPv6 in Docker (after the OPS-1 deploy, D-38). With Docker's IPv6 on for the `edge` network, IPv6 clients would reach Caddy with their own address, and the per-address limit would count them too. It changes the auth server's host, so it waits for a maintenance window.
- Separating a member's own entries from their shares in a family category's personal cash-flow line (after the F6c deploy, D-40).
- Settlement sides on an account that requires a counterparty, or on FX_EXCHANGE, in the settlement forms (D-162). Today only the import writes them (D-148, D-71).
- The dashboard's two columns from 80rem (D-609, after UX-14's review; D-593 amended when done), proposed for the next stage with code. Between 64 and 80rem the main column is too narrow for "Cash flow by category": at 1024 px its headers wrap into narrow columns, "Year earlier Nov 1 – 12, 2025" over five lines. Two columns from 80rem instead of 64rem; under 80rem one column, in the same order. `07-dashboard`'s step 13 follows (its 1024 px case then one column). Closed by D-654 (UX-16).
- The stale JSDoc above `rateSourceText` (D-609, after UX-14's review), proposed for the next stage with code: the comment of the removed `usedRateText` ("A rate a figure in the base currency used, in words …") stayed above `rateSourceText`'s own in `frontend/src/dashboard.ts`; remove it. Closed by D-654 (UX-16).
- The emails' plain link (D-639, after UX-15's review; D-626 amended when done), proposed for a later stage: drop the plain link under the button from auth_server's `email/html/template.ftl`. Keycloak's action links are about 800 characters and fill half the email; the plain-text part keeps the link.
- The emails' footer (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: "Sent by Finance Tracker's sign-in. If you didn't expect it, you can ignore it." repeats each body's own "ignore" sentence; a footer that only names the service.
- The descriptions on "Select login method" (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: at "Try Another Way" during a first sign-in through Google or GitHub into an existing account, each way's description is dimmer than `--muted` in Blue and Dark; a PatternFly variable isn't mapped to a token in auth_server's `login.css`.
- A check of the Keycloak theme's tokens (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: that auth_server's `tokens.css` still equals `frontend/src/index.css`'s tokens. D-625 copies them by hand, and nothing notices a difference.
- The theme in the privacy policy (D-639, after UX-15's review), proposed for a later stage: the policy doesn't say that the chosen theme is also kept in the browser's local storage (since UX-10); one phrase beside the session storage paragraph.
- The Node of the laptop's `frontend/` (D-672, UX-16's second commit), proposed for a later stage: its default Node v22.19.0's npm 11.6.2 writes a lock that CI's npm refuses. A `.nvmrc` naming CI's Node (and `engines` in `package.json`) would make the laptop's npm CI's; not done in UX-16's second commit, whose scope leaves `package.json` alone. D-669's check stays either way.
- A value after a marker past its bar's end (D-678, after UX-16's review), proposed for a later stage, the owner to decide once he has seen the chart on his own data: where a comparison's marker lies past its bar's end, the value follows the marker (D-650, D-658) and stands apart from its bar: on "Last 3 months" compared with the previous period, Oct 2026's income €2,540.00 stands after its comparison's marker, about 80 px past the bar. Kept by D-713 (UX-17-doc), the owner to decide: since UX-17 it shows on the demo's "Past year" right after the load and on the landing page's `demo-dashboard.webp` (on 2026-11-13's demo, Mar and Jul 2026's income values after the previous year's marker).
- The hover panel's "(—)" (D-678, after UX-16's review), proposed for a later stage, the owner to decide as above: when the comparison's month has no figure, the panel shows "Income (—)", as before UX-16; no change shown there instead. Closed by D-687 (UX-17): a comparison month with no figure in either series is left out of the panel, and a series without a figure shows "—" alone.
- `DemoLedger`'s Javadoc of `varied` and `grown` (D-712, after UX-17's review), for the next stage with code: it says the step is "of the year and month", but the month term is 0 modulo 7, so the step depends on the year (and each item's salt) only.
